# tool-api (K-09): the Tool API

**Status: v1, v1.1, v1.2, v1.3 and v1.4 frozen** (`frozen.json`: `{"frozen": ["v1", "v1.1", "v1.2", "v1.3", "v1.4"]}`). v1.4, a fourth minor version that only adds, holds journey 6's calls, booked (T-053; section "v1.4" below; F16). v1.3, a third minor version that only adds, holds journey 5's calls, money before (T-052; section "v1.3" below; F16); its second round, before the merge, closed the first verification's points under the lead's rulings (DL-252; section "v1.3", "Round 2"), and its third the second verification's one point, a pack charged with no bound yes (DL-257; section "v1.3", "Round 3"). T-009 drafted the turn face and the shared core; T-018 added the setup calls journeys 1 and 2 use, the exchanges, the named rules and the fixtures, and froze v1 (02-contracts K-09, "Freeze": F5). Its second and third rounds, before the merge, closed the points of the first and second verifications (change log). v1.1, a minor version that only adds, holds journey 3's calls (T-050; section "v1.1" below; F15), and v1.2, another, holds journey 4's (T-051; section "v1.2" below; F16); v1.2's second round, before the merge, closed the first verification's points under the lead's rulings (DL-162; section "v1.2", "Round 2"), its third the second verification's (DL-171; section "v1.2", "Round 3"), and its fourth, in this README alone, the third verification's (DL-175; section "v1.2", "Round 4"). Each freeze takes effect when its task merges, and the merge waits for every review below (build prompt 6.2, 6.4, 6.5).

**Owner:** the architect. **Schema:** `tool-api.v1.schema.json` (JSON Schema 2020-12, `$id` `https://rosie.invalid/contracts/tool-api/tool-api.v1.schema.json`). Each call's request, response and errors are `$defs` named `<call>.request`, `<call>.response` and `<call>.errors` (02-contracts section 2 item 2), `$defs/callName` lists the calls, and `<call>.exchange` pairs one request with its outcome (`given`, `request`, `outcome`), as K-05's exchanges do. The root is any one exchange, so the type generator writes a type for every call. **Checked by:** `npm run contracts:check` (the schema compiles in Ajv's strict 2020-12 mode, every `$ref` resolves, every fixture in `fixtures/index.json` is accepted or refused as listed, and the named rules pass). **Contract test:** `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts` (QA, T-088). **Mock:** the Tool API mock (C-186, T-510) answers a request that matches an exchange's request with that exchange's outcome and fails loudly on anything else (02-contracts section 2 item 9). A change after the freeze is a new minor version file, `tool-api.v1.1.schema.json`, with new fixtures (build prompt 6.4); the frozen-contract guard fails any edit to v1's schema, its fixtures or their index entries. **v1.1:** `tool-api.v1.1.schema.json` (`$id` `https://rosie.invalid/contracts/tool-api/tool-api.v1.1.schema.json`), journey 3's calls (section "v1.1"); contract test `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.1.test.ts` (QA, T-117); **v1.2:** `tool-api.v1.2.schema.json` (`$id` `https://rosie.invalid/contracts/tool-api/tool-api.v1.2.schema.json`), journey 4's calls (section "v1.2"); contract test `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.2.test.ts` (QA, T-118); **v1.3:** `tool-api.v1.3.schema.json` (`$id` `https://rosie.invalid/contracts/tool-api/tool-api.v1.3.schema.json`), journey 5's calls (section "v1.3"); contract test `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.3.test.ts` (QA, T-119); **v1.4:** `tool-api.v1.4.schema.json` (`$id` `https://rosie.invalid/contracts/tool-api/tool-api.v1.4.schema.json`), journey 6's calls (section "v1.4"); contract test `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.4.test.ts` (QA, T-120); the next change is `tool-api.v1.5.schema.json` (journey 7).

## What it defines

The calls that own every read and write (arch 02 line 139), "generated from the master's needs and gives keys" (arch 12 line 590). The same calls serve texts, secure pages and outside AIs under the same limits; free text from pages and AIs gets the safety read first (arch 02 line 129); row-level security scopes every call by the caller's business (arch 10 line 546; K-04 v1 rule 4). Every request carries a `caller` (`$defs/caller`): who calls (a turn, a page, an agent, the console, a job, a clock firing, or the handle actor writing on return), the business it runs under, the person it acts for (stated per call as the session's `rosie.person_id`, which K-22 v1's row predicates read: K-22 v1 rule 4; none for a sign-in call until its code is right), the key a repeat is known by, and the trace it joins (K-08).

Every field's `description` names its source (02-contracts section 2 item 4). "arch NN line N" are the lines of `scratch/text/architecture.txt`, as `ops/plan/02-contracts.md` cites them. Frozen contracts are reused by relative `$ref` where a call carries their shapes: K-01 v1 (`stepId`), K-02 v1 (`context`, `text`, `contextAndDecide`, `plannerOutput`, `safetyResult`, `safetyOutcome`, `language`), K-03 v1 (`composeInput`, `rowPlan`, `finalParts`, `checkResult`), K-05 v1 (`turn`, `commitResult`, `rowRef`, `sendClass`, `phoneNumber` and its errors), K-07 v1 (`effectKind`, `rowState`), K-08 v1 (`traceId`, `limitResult`) and the shared formats (`id`, `turnId`, `utc`, `money`). The table parts (K-04 core, K-23, K-24, K-26, K-29, K-33, K-34, K-36, K-37) are table lists, so the calls name their tables, columns and closed lists in words and enums, never by `$ref`. K-22 v1's proof codes and the call's person are mapped as frozen ("Proof codes in K-22 v1"); the rest of K-22, K-25 and K-28 (frozen on main after this contract was first written) and the parts not yet frozen (K-27, K-35, and the ports K-12 to K-16) are named in words only (open gap 2).

**CQ-25 (settled):** decide reaches the model in two calls a read, made by the AI gateway (T-361). The turn face passes K-02 v1's context to decide, and takes K-02 v1's decide result back, unchanged; the wire form is C-115's and C-121's, not the Tool API's.

### The turn face (called by the handle actor and the AI layer)

| Call              | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Source                                                                |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `buildFrontier`   | The turn, its targets from the guard and the pinned bundle give K-02's context; each fact once from its home; a second value for one key refused and the step flagged; over 60 choices, other bookings collapse                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | arch 02 line 117; arch 03 lines 157 to 167                            |
| `closestEntries`  | Nothing fits: the 3 closest entry steps join the choices, and the turn's one re-read runs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | arch 03 line 189; Q-42                                                |
| `gateAndCommit`   | Limits 1 to 6 on each agreed part; per part, committed (with the keys its step gave) or the failed limit and its outcome (ask, refuse, wait), which stops only that choice and what depends on it; one commit under the turn id; a value that fails its words before decide's one re-read is refused before any write (`valueFailsWords`); a clock's firing takes the same call, as a clock turn or with no turn, and answers `fired` | arch 02 line 121; arch 06 lines 295 to 309; arch 10 line 508; NQ-RT-4 |
| `plannerVerbs`    | The planner's five verbs, carried inside `gateAndCommit` (one commit per turn); every such turn is a gap case                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | arch 06 line 291; arch 03 line 189                                    |
| `composeInput`    | An outbox row gives K-03's composer input; an email copy takes its text row's plan                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | arch 07 line 397                                                      |
| `finalizeMessage` | An outbox row and its plan give the final parts after options, fixed lines, slots, the claim check and the message checks; or the failed checks for one rewrite; then the fallback; then "I've passed this on."                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | arch 02 line 123; arch 07 lines 405 to 417                            |

### The shared core (M3b: "the any-time rows every chat needs from its first text")

| Call                                      | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Source                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `recordSafetyOutcome`                     | The outcome row, its hold, the checked line (send class safety) and who is told; for a page or an AI, the safety message back and nothing books                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | arch 06 lines 315 to 329; arch 02 lines 111, 129; A3-22 to A3-26, A3-48 to A3-50, B3-17 |
| `releaseSafetyHold`                       | Release by the hold's rule: at once, when private, after a clear "we're safe" with a fresh read-back; a threat never                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | arch 06 lines 319 to 327; A3-24, A3-25, A3-48, A3-50                                    |
| `recordStopOrStart`                       | STOP, START and a work phone's answer to "which texts?"; CANCEL also flags the visit; written on return when PostgreSQL was down. Each binds to the number it came from: START only from that phone's own START text (its event, in its turn or written on return by its actor), never from a page, an agent, the console or a job; a stop for another number changes nothing (`notFromThatPhone`) | RULES 7.2, 7.4, 7.5, 7.7; MC C1; arch 11 line 566                                       |
| `setRecordFlag`                           | Set and clear private, paused and deceased (stopped is `recordStopOrStart`'s); a pause arms the call-off clocks                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | arch 03 lines 175 to 177; S11-62                                                        |
| `handOff`, `answerHandOff`                | Hand to the owner, then Rosie's team (crisis words at once; an emergency or a threat after 5 minutes; any other after 2 days); a report about the owner or staff to the team only                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | arch 06 line 329; S11-19; S13-29 rules 53 to 57                                         |

| `sendsFailing`, `textingBack`             | Texting down: the next way after 15 minutes, or the owner gets the number to call; once texts work, one short "all set"                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | S12-09, S12-10; arch 02 line 125                                                        |

"Booking is paused" for AIs and the booking page while PostgreSQL is down (S12-12; arch 11 line 566) is not a call: it is `$defs/bookingPausedError`, which the calls a page or an agent reaches can answer.

## Reads, repeats and time budgets (02-contracts section 2 item 2)

Every call reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached**: each is a decision, a write, or a screen that acts at once ("Decision reads go to the primary, uncached": arch 02 line 117; arch 10 lines 504, 538). None is search or a report, so none reads the replica. The same three facts open each `<call>.request` description in the schema.

| Call                                      | In a turn or alone                                                                                                                                     | Repeating it                                                                                                                                                                                | Time budget                                                                                                          |
| ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `buildFrontier`                           | turn face                                                                                                                                              | Safe: a read; a flagged step is reported, not written                                                                                                                                       | At most 150 ms (arch 04 line 215)                                                                                    |
| `closestEntries`                          | turn face                                                                                                                                              | Safe: a read; once per turn (`searchedAlready`)                                                                                                                                             | None in the sources; 200 ms proposed (default 3)                                                                     |
| `gateAndCommit`                           | turn face; a clock's firing (a clock turn, or none for the sweep)                                                                                      | Safe: the turn id is the key, a clock turn's being its clock row's (or the clock row with no turn); a repeat returns the first commit or firing (`repeated`; K-05 v1 commitTurn, fireClock) | At most 300 ms (arch 04 line 223)                                                                                    |
| `plannerVerbs`                            | inside `gateAndCommit`                                                                                                                                 | As `gateAndCommit`                                                                                                                                                                          | Inside its 300 ms                                                                                                    |
| `composeInput`                            | turn face                                                                                                                                              | Safe: a read                                                                                                                                                                                | None; inside "Write the reply, or one rewrite: 2–3 s" (arch 04 line 225)                                             |
| `finalizeMessage`                         | turn face                                                                                                                                              | Safe: a row with a plan keeps it (`alreadyPlanned`; K-05 v1 writePlan)                                                                                                                      | None; the checks are code, inside the same 2–3 s                                                                     |
| `recordSafetyOutcome`                     | turn (each text), or alone (page, agent)                                                                                                               | Safe: the event id is the key; one outcome row per text                                                                                                                                     | The checked line first, within 5 s (NQ-RT-9); the read is 2 s a model, 4 s in all (arch 02 line 111), the AI layer's |
| `releaseSafetyHold`                       | either                                                                                                                                                 | Safe: a released hold answers `repeated`                                                                                                                                                    | None                                                                                                                 |
| `recordStopOrStart`                       | turn (a work phone's no answer: default 24's clock turn inside `gateAndCommit`), or alone (actor on return; a page's STOP for the caller's own number) | Safe: the turn or clock row is the key; START names its text's event                                                                                                                        | None                                                                                                                 |
| `setRecordFlag`                           | either                                                                                                                                                 | Safe: a flag already as asked answers `repeated`; a clear compares the version                                                                                                              | None                                                                                                                 |
| `handOff`                                 | either                                                                                                                                                 | Safe: the turn or request id is the key                                                                                                                                                     | Crisis words at once; emergency or threat 5 minutes; other 2 days (arch 06 line 329; S11-19)                         |
| `answerHandOff`                           | either                                                                                                                                                 | Safe: `repeated`                                                                                                                                                                            | None                                                                                                                 |
| `pageTeam`                                | either (a repeat page from its clock)                                                                                                                  | Safe: the request id, or the clock row, is the key                                                                                                                                          | Every 5 minutes until acknowledged (arch 06 line 329, DD)                                                            |
| `acknowledgePage`                         | either (CQ-32)                                                                                                                                         | Safe: `repeated`                                                                                                                                                                            | None                                                                                                                 |
| `openReviewItem`                          | either                                                                                                                                                 | Safe: the turn or request id is the key                                                                                                                                                     | The team's answer in 1 business day, that day for a harm report                                                      |
| `decideReviewItem`                        | alone (console)                                                                                                                                        | Safe: a decided item answers `repeated`                                                                                                                                                     | None                                                                                                                 |
| `enterDoor`                               | turn only                                                                                                                                              | As `gateAndCommit`                                                                                                                                                                          | Inside its 300 ms                                                                                                    |
| `sendFixedLine`                           | either (actor on return)                                                                                                                               | Safe: once per text                                                                                                                                                                         | None                                                                                                                 |
| `sendsFailing`                            | alone (dispatcher)                                                                                                                                     | Safe: a failed row answers `repeated`                                                                                                                                                       | After 15 minutes of tries (arch 02 line 125)                                                                         |
| `textingBack`                             | alone (dispatcher)                                                                                                                                     | Safe: one "all set" per person per outage                                                                                                                                                   | None                                                                                                                 |
| `calendarLinkDropped`, `calendarLinkBack` | alone (calendar job, page)                                                                                                                             | Safe: `repeated`                                                                                                                                                                            | The reminder after 24 hours (S12-14)                                                                                 |
| `sendProofCode`                           | either                                                                                                                                                 | Safe: a repeat returns the same code row                                                                                                                                                    | The code lives 10 minutes (RULES 6.2)                                                                                |
| `proveCode`                               | either                                                                                                                                                 | Keyed: the event or request id; each new event counts a try                                                                                                                                 | 10 minutes; 5 wrong give a 15-minute pause (RULES 6.2)                                                               |
| `issueChatLink`                           | either                                                                                                                                                 | Safe: an unused link for the same purpose, booking and mobile comes back, `given_via` extended                                                                                              | None; a staff invite's owner reminder after 48 hours (S2-07)                                                         |
| `matchKinds`                              | either (frontier builder; registration screen 1)                                                                                                       | Safe: a read                                                                                                                                                                                | None in the sources; 200 ms proposed (default 20)                                                                    |
| `lookupListing`                           | either (frontier builder; registration screen 2)                                                                                                       | Safe: a read                                                                                                                                                                                | None in the sources; the Places port's own                                                                           |
| `saveSetupStep`                           | turn (the choice's effect; S2-05 as a core action), or alone                                                                                           | Safe: the turn or request id is the key; a repeat answers `repeated`                                                                                                                        | In a turn, inside `gateAndCommit`'s 300 ms; alone, none                                                              |
| `readSetup`                               | alone (pages, through K-21)                                                                                                                            | Safe: a read                                                                                                                                                                                | None                                                                                                                 |

Every call can also answer `transient` (K-05 v1's: a serialization failure, a deadlock or a lost connection): nothing was written, and the same request may be sent again.

### In a turn or alone

A turn commits once (arch 02 line 121; arch 10 line 508). So a shared-core call made in a turn (a STOP, a safety outcome, a door, an owner's answer to a hand-off, a staff member's ready text) rides inside `gateAndCommit`: its request in `request.core` (`$defs/coreAction`, with `caller.kind` turn) and its response or error in `response.core` (`$defs/coreResult`). A setup step's write in a turn is the effect code adds for the choice decide picked, after limits 1 to 6 pass, and the part's result names the keys it gave (`partResult.gives`). The one setup step that rides as a core action is S2-05's ready text: `coreAction`'s saveSetupStep takes `$defs/saveSetupStepInTurn`, whose step is only `SetupS2_05`, so no consent, money or team write (S1-25's fee_ok, S1-27, S1-29, S2-02, S2-24) can commit from a turn's core outside the limits (`invalid/refuse-core-setup-step-not-ready-text.json`). A refused core action stops only itself. A call made alone (by a page, an agent, the console, a job or a clock firing with no handle) commits once under `caller.requestId`, which waits for K-05's commit with no turn (CQ-44's default). An exchange whose `caller.kind` is turn and whose call is not `gateAndCommit` shows a core action or a step's write as it rides inside a turn's commit: the same request and result, which the mock answers the same way.

### A clock's firing (`gateAndCommit`, `input.kind` clock)

QUESTIONS.md NQ-RT-4: a clock is a turn on the handle's actor, or a sweep job for a row with no handle, which calls `gateAndCommit` with the clock branch, no decide reads and the firing's own trace id (CQ-06), and commits through K-05 v1 `fireClock`.

- **The turn:** for a row a handle owns, a clock turn on that handle: its one event id is the clock row's id (K-05 v1 default 9: K-04 needs one, and a firing has no text), its id that event id's hash, as every turn's is (CQ-09), so one row's firing has one turn id; its trace id is the firing's own; `caller.kind` turn, with `caller.turnId` and `caller.requestId` its id. With no turn (a row no handle owns, or a firing whose writes hold no outbox row: K-05 v1 `fireClock.request.turn`), `turn` is null and `caller.kind` is clockFiring, `caller.turnId` null and `caller.requestId` the clock row's id. Only a clock's firing may have a null turn (`invalid/refuse-texts-without-turn.json`); the named rule `clockTurn` checks the rest.
- **The answer:** `fired` (`$defs/gateAndCommit.fired`, as K-05 v1 `fireClock.response`): the clock row's version, the firing's time, the turn id or null, `repeated`, and what the branch wrote. A repeat, such as the minute sweep after the alarm (they race by design: arch 10 line 540), returns the first firing with `repeated` true, never an error. A row still waiting or cancelled answers `notArmed`, one not yet due `notDue` (except staging's Fire now), and one whose own version moved the clock row's `versionChanged` (`gateAndCommit.errors`). A row the branch read that changed reloads once and runs the branch again, as limit 4 does (arch 06 line 305).
- **Fixtures:** `clock-staff-invite-48h.json` (S2-05's "No reply" after 48 hours, S2-07 under MC C9: Sam is told once and given Riley's link again) and `clock-staff-invite-48h-repeat.json` (the same firing again: `repeated`).

### Limits 1 to 6 at the gate (`gateAndCommit`)

Each part of a committed turn comes back as `$defs/partResult`: `committed`, with the keys its step gave; `stopped`, with `stoppedBy`, the failed limit and its outcome; `dependsOnStopped`; `held` by a safety hold; `asked` (decide ended it in one question); or `noAction`. `limits` lists each limit's result as K-08 v1's `limitResult`, which the gate span logs. Each outcome's kind is one of `ask`, `refuse` and `wait` (`$defs/gateOutcome`), and each limit's actions are an enum in `limit1Outcome` to `limit6Outcome`, one kind per action. Each limit fixture states its outcome in `given.stated`, and the named rule `statedOutcome` checks the fixture gives it:

| Limit         | Action               | Kind   | What happens (arch 06)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Fixture                                                           |
| ------------- | -------------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1 Allowed     | `proofCodeSent`      | wait   | Unproven sender: a code to the contact on file, in words that never confirm a booking exists (line 299); the outcome names the code (`proofCodeId`) and the question in the sender's own chat that takes it typed back (`openQuestionId`; S13-45), and `proveCode` then names the turn to decide again (`redecideTurnId`) | `commit-limit-1-unproven-sender.json`, `limit-1-code-proven.json` |
| 1 Allowed     | `askedDecider`       | ask    | Wrong person: a question in the decider's own chat (line 299)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |                                                                   |
| 2 Owner rules | `saidWhatRuleAllows` | refuse | Rosie says what the rule allows; nothing is saved (line 301)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `commit-limit-2-owner-rule.json`                                  |
| 3 Exact yes   | `freshReadBack`      | ask    | A fresh read-back, once (line 303)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `commit-limit-3-no-exact-yes.json`                                |
| 3 Exact yes   | `safeDefault`        | refuse | Then the step's safe default, and the owner is told (line 303)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |                                                                   |
| 4 Versions    | `freshYes`           | ask    | Changed but still possible: today's facts and a fresh yes (line 305)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `commit-limit-4-changed.json`                                     |
| 4 Versions    | `saidWhatChanged`    | refuse | No longer possible: say what changed and offer the current choices (line 305)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |                                                                   |
| 4 Versions    | `toPerson`           | wait   | A second conflict on one question: a person (line 305)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |                                                                   |
| 5 Reserved    | `nextOpenTimes`      | ask    | The next open times (line 307)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `commit-limit-5-taken.json`                                       |
| 5 Reserved    | `nothingBooked`      | refuse | No pick in 10 minutes on the page, or 2 of their text hours: nothing booked (line 307; A6-11)                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |                                                                   |
| 6 Values      | `oneQuestion`        | ask    | Still failing after decide's one Sonnet re-read (`reread` true), one question (lines 289, 309)                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `commit-limit-6-value-fails.json`                                 |
| 6 Values      | `nothingChanged`     | refuse | Then nothing changes (line 309)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |                                                                   |

Limit 6 runs its outcome only after decide's one Sonnet re-read (arch 02 line 119: "a value that fails its words: one Sonnet re-read per turn. Still unclear: one question"; arch 06 lines 289, 309; Q-41). While `decide.reread` is false, a value that fails its words is refused before any write, the turn's core actions included, with `valueFailsWords` naming the parts and their fields (`gateAndCommit.errors`): C-116 runs the re-read and the turn loop sends the turn to the gate again (`commit-limit-6-reread-first.json`, then `commit-limit-6-value-fails.json`). The named rule `rereadBeforeQuestion` refuses a limit 6 stop with `reread` false (`invalid/refuse-limit-6-question-before-reread.json`), and `statedOutcome` checks the same. A bare STOP never waits for that re-read: the runtime runs a text that is only an opt-out word in its own core-only turn (`stop-recorded.json`), never joined to a turn of decided texts, so `valueFailsWords` never holds a STOP back (default 35).

Limit 4 reloads once before it fails: nothing shown changed means the choice commits (line 305). A K-05 v1 `versionChanged` or `reservationLost` from the commit is turned into limit 4's or limit 5's outcome for the parts it touches, never returned as an error. Limits 7 (claim check) and 8 (at send) are `finalizeMessage`'s and the dispatcher's.

## Proof codes in K-22 v1

K-22 v1 (T-031) froze on main after this contract was first written, and this branch is rebased on it. Each code `sendProofCode` sends, and the one limit 1 sends inside `gateAndCommit`, is one K-22 v1 `proof_codes` row: only its keyed hash, never the code (BP rule 7). Its columns come from the request as below; the person is `forPersonId`, never taken from `caller.personId` (default 34).

| `purpose`        | K-22 v1 `purpose` | `person_id` (`forPersonId`, set by the server, never the browser)                                                                                                                 | `sent_to_handle_id`                                                                                 | `caller.personId`                   |
| ---------------- | ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| `signIn`         | `sign_in`         | The account the typed mobile or email finds, on the runtime side (S11-52; K-22 v1 README, "Every path that binds a person")                                                       | `contactHandleId`: that account's email or mobile                                                   | null until the code is right        |
| `emailCheck`     | `owner_email`     | The person the secure page's own signed link was made for (K-21): S1-10's link names the owner, or on the helper path the helper; S1-77's link the owner (MASTER-FLOW-A line 182) | The typed address's handle, which the call writes when new                                          | null until the code is right        |
| `unprovenSender` | `link_record`     | The decider whose contact on file gets the code (limit 1: arch 06 line 299)                                                                                                       | `contactHandleId`: that contact on file                                                             | the turn's                          |
| `newContact`     | `contact_change`  | The person whose new phone or email it proves (limit 3: arch 06 line 303; D75)                                                                                                    | `contactHandleId`: the new contact                                                                  | the turn's                          |
| `control`        | `listing_control` | null: the code proves a business (`proof_codes_who`)                                                                                                                              | The listing email's handle (K-23 v1 `business_setup.listing_email`), which the call writes when new | the caller's (a turn's or a page's) |

- **`business_id`:** null for `sign_in`, which covers every business of the person; for every other purpose `caller.businessId`, the business the code's words name (S13-45: "Your Lee's Barbershop code is 4829"). **`channel`:** email on an email route (`emailOnFile`, `listingEmail`, `typedEmail`), text on `textFromMobile`. **`expires_at`** is `sent_at` plus 10 minutes, and the counts, pause and use start at their defaults (K-22 v1 rule 5).
- **The person is kept on the server:** the code id and its person go from the send to the check on the server, never through the page, which holds at most an opaque attempt id (K-22 v1 rule 4). `proveCode` checks by the code id (`check_proof_code`); once the code is right, a sign-in acts for that person in the same transaction, and for `emailCheck` the runtime side sets that person's `accounts.email_proven_at` and `proveCode` links the handle (K-04 v1 `person_handles`).
- **The schema holds it** (`sendProofCode.request`'s `dependentSchemas` on `purpose`): `forPersonId` is null for `control` and an id for every other purpose (`invalid/refuse-email-check-without-person.json`), and `caller.personId` is null on a `signIn` or `emailCheck` send (`invalid/refuse-sign-in-acting-for-person.json`).
- **K-22 v1 purposes with no K-09 v1 purpose:** `new_device`, `closing_or_export` and `hand_over` (open gap 24).

## The event kinds each call writes (K-08 v1.1)

Every write appends event-log rows of K-08's closed list; the calls name K-08 v1.1 (T-1077), where the kinds the shared core writes were added. The turn face and `recordStopOrStart`, `setRecordFlag`, `enterDoor`, `sendFixedLine`, `sendsFailing`, `textingBack` and `finalizeMessage` write only v1 kinds; `recordSafetyOutcome`, `releaseSafetyHold`, `handOff`, `answerHandOff`, `pageTeam`, `acknowledgePage`, `openReviewItem`, `decideReviewItem`, `issueChatLink`, `sendProofCode`, `proveCode` and the planner's gap case write v1.1's added kinds as its section "v1.1" states them. On `sendProofCode`'s email routes the email row's final parts hold the code, so that row's entry leaves the final parts out and the code never reaches the log. `saveSetupStep` writes v1 kinds for the core rows (`business.status_changed`, `person.created`, `person_role.added`, `phone.contact_added`, `consent.given`, the outbox, open question and clock kinds); its other setup writes have no kind in K-08 v1.1 (open gaps), as do `calendarLinkDropped` and `calendarLinkBack`. `sendProofCode`'s typedEmail route writes v1's `handle.created`; the person_handles row `proveCode` adds for it, and the rows a review decision writes back (K-23 business_setup, K-24 new_work_drafts), have no kind in K-08 v1.1 either (open gap 1).

## Named rules (`tool-api.rules.mjs`)

What an exchange states that one JSON Schema cannot check across its parts. `npm run contracts:check` runs the rule an index entry names on a fixture that has passed its schema; QA's contract test and the mock read the same statements. Rules are only ever added.

- **`statedOutcome`:** a gate exchange whose `given.stated` names a limit stops exactly one part, by that limit, with that kind of outcome and that action, and logs that limit as failed with that action on the part's limits (02-contracts K-09 "Contract test": each limit fixture gives its stated outcome).
- **`givesStepKeys`:** an exchange whose `given.stated` names a step writes every key that step gives (`STEP_GIVES`, from `A-S1.md` and `A-S2.md`), and its outcome wrote exactly the stated keys; a stated key beyond the step's own must be one an automatic step its walk passes gives (S1-11 after S1-02: arch 03 line 191). 02-contracts K-09: every journey 1 and 2 call writes the key its step gives.
- **`rereadBeforeQuestion`:** a gate exchange on decided texts answers `valueFailsWords` only while `decide.reread` is false, naming parts decide sent to the gate and fields those parts carry, and stops a part by limit 6 only when `reread` is true (arch 02 line 119; arch 06 lines 289, 309; Q-41). `statedOutcome` runs the same check on the limit fixtures.
- **`clockTurn`:** a clock's firing is a clock turn (`caller.kind` turn, `caller.turnId` and `requestId` the turn's id, its one event id the clock row's id, its trace id the caller's) or has no turn (`caller.kind` clockFiring, `caller.turnId` null, `requestId` the clock row's id); it carries no safety outcome, and it answers `fired` for that row and that turn, never `committed` (NQ-RT-4; K-05 v1 fireClock and default 9; CQ-06).

## Producers and consumers (02-contracts K-09)

- **Producer:** action-layer engineer: C-074 Tool API, with C-075 to C-112 and C-130 to C-138 as they land.
- **Consumers:** runtime engineer: C-046 handle actor (the turn loop), C-047 safety read on arrival, C-051 guard, C-052 guard fixed replies, C-174 AI provider down, C-175 PostgreSQL down; AI engineer: C-114 context bundle, C-117 planner, C-118 composer; frontend engineer, through K-21: C-185 to C-205; architect: C-186 Tool API mock; action-layer engineer: C-110 Agent API; QA: C-140 eval harness, C-147 scenario tests, C-148 end-to-end tests.
- **Frozen before it, read here:** K-01 v1, K-02 v1, K-03 v1, K-04 v1, K-05 v1 and v1.1, K-07 v1, K-08 v1 and v1.1, K-17 v1, K-23 v1, K-24 v1, K-26 v1, K-29 v1, K-33 v1, K-34 v1, K-36 v1, K-37 v1, and K-22 v1 for its proof codes and the call's person only ("Proof codes in K-22 v1"). The tasks that build its journey 1 and 2 calls also read the setup parts frozen at F14 (K-22, K-25, K-27, K-35 among them), each adopted by a K-09 minor version (open gaps).
- **Contract test:** `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts` (QA, T-088): the mock, the action layer and each caller agree on every exchange; each limit fixture gives its stated outcome; each setup fixture writes its step's keys; limit 6 waits for decide's re-read (`rereadBeforeQuestion`); each clock fixture keeps the clock turn's rule (`clockTurn`).

## Fixtures (`fixtures/`, listed in `fixtures/index.json`)

Every fixture is one `<call>.exchange` (`given`: the seeds and fixtures it runs after, the clock, what it states; `request`; `outcome`), except the refused ones in `fixtures/invalid/`, which name the definition that refuses them.

- **The turn face:** `build-frontier-s1-02.json`, `closest-entries.json`, `commit-s1-02.json` (S1-02 committed, the walk through S1-11: kinds and the 11 pf keys), the six `commit-limit-*.json` above with `commit-limit-6-reread-first.json` (refused before any write until the re-read) and `limit-1-code-proven.json` (limit 1's code typed back), `clock-staff-invite-48h.json` and `clock-staff-invite-48h-repeat.json` (a clock's firing), `compose-input-s1-02.json`, `finalize-pass.json`, `finalize-rewrite.json`, `finalize-passed-on.json`. The contexts, decide results, inputs, plans and check results are K-02's and K-03's fixtures (`context-s1-02.json`, `decide-s1-02-choose.json`, `safety-threat.json`, `input-s1-02.json`, `plan-s1-02.json`, `check-fallback.json`, `check-passed-on.json`), word for word where a fixture reuses one.
- **The shared core:** `safety-outcome-hold.json`, `stop-recorded.json` (a core-only turn), `start-recorded.json` (on return), `stop-other-number.json` (a stop for another number: `notFromThatPhone`), `flag-paused.json`, `page-team.json`, `review-item.json`, `new-work-approved.json` (the team's decision written back to the draft), `booking-paused.json`, `proof-code-email.json`, `proof-code-text-from-mobile.json`, `chat-link-after-contact-add.json`, `chat-link-contact-add-failed.json`.
- **Journeys 1 and 2:** `match-kinds.json`, `listing-lookup.json`, `email-check-typed.json` and `email-check-proven.json` (the owner's email, FS row B13), `id-upload-confirmed.json`, `control-code-email.json`, `control-team-review.json`, `control-team-decided.json` (the team's control decision written back to K-23), `fee-consent.json`, `go-live-check.json`, `add-team.json`, `staff-join-link.json`, `staff-join-ready-text.json`, `staff-join-other-mobile.json`, `take-bookings.json`, and one `setup-*.json` per remaining key-giving step (S1-04, S1-09, S1-23, S1-12, S1-13, S1-84, S1-24, S1-27, S1-29, S1-35, S2-06, S2-11, S2-15, S2-27). S1-01's thread, S1-21's rules and S1-81's spaces have no fixture (open gaps).
- **Refused:** `invalid/refuse-team-without-tick.json` (S2-02 without the owner's tick: Q-08), `invalid/refuse-limit-outcome-wrong-kind.json` (an action with another limit's kind), `invalid/refuse-start-from-page.json` (START from a page), `invalid/refuse-start-without-text.json` (START naming no inbound text), `invalid/refuse-core-setup-step-not-ready-text.json` (S1-25's consent as a turn's core action), `invalid/refuse-texts-without-turn.json` (a turn on texts with no turn row), `invalid/refuse-email-check-without-person.json` (S1-10's owner email code naming no person), `invalid/refuse-sign-in-acting-for-person.json` (a sign-in code sent by a call acting for a person) and, by the rule `rereadBeforeQuestion`, `invalid/refuse-limit-6-question-before-reread.json` (limit 6's question before the re-read). Each is refused for its one fault: with that fault fixed it is accepted (checked 2026-11-07).

Names and values come only from the master's sample cast and fixture set v1's demo shops (Sam Lee and Lee's Barbershop, Lee Home Services, Alex M., Ben T., Riley, Avery, Pat; S1 and S2 steps; October 2026), never a real person (rule 9; MC C6). Phone numbers are in the fictional 555-01xx range, websites and emails on the reserved `.example` domain, Rosie's team member in the decision fixtures is a placeholder id with no name (the sample cast names no one on Rosie's team: K-37 open gaps), and every code, license number, Stripe id and object key is a placeholder; no fixture holds a token or a hash value beyond the frozen contracts' own placeholders (the bundle id and prefix hash of K-01's bundle v0). Row, turn, trace and event ids continue K-02's, K-03's, K-08's and the table parts' seeds.

## Defaults used (T-009)

1. **Q-03:** a chat link is shown only once the mobile is a Linq contact; until contacts/add has worked `issueChatLink` answers `waitingForContact` (the effect row, CQ-04), and if it failed `contactAddFailed`, with the booking page, email or the person's own AI as the other ways.
2. **Q-18:** Rosie's team is Jay and the CTO until launch: they get pages, decide review items and follow up harm reports (`notTeam` otherwise); on staging only roster test phones and the test console (BP 9.4).
3. **New, `closestEntries`' budget:** no source gives one. Proposed: at most 200 ms, the "Search, one indexed query" budget (arch 04 line 217, DD).
4. **Q-40 and Q-42:** an any-time row with no handler step is not offered as a choice (K-02 v1 default 4); the meaning-match index is named by its spike, and while it cannot be read `closestEntries` answers `indexUnavailable` and the turn goes on to the planner (arch 03 line 189).
5. **New, a text's safety outcome:** each text's outcome, No included, is a `recordSafetyOutcome` core action of the turn its text joins, since K-34 v1 writes one outcome row per text with that turn's id. With danger, the actor closes the batch at once, so the checked line goes first as an outbox row of send class safety within 5 s (NQ-RT-9).
6. **New, stop and start replies:** while PostgreSQL is up, the STOP, START and HELP replies are outbox rows of send class quick_reply, a reply in the same turn (Q-15); only while it is down does the actor send them straight through Linq, written on return through `sentByActor` (arch 11 line 566).
7. **CQ-05:** text hours, and a work phone's "next text hour" (RULES 7.7), are read in the business's zone for owners and staff, and in the visit's zone, else the business's, for customers.
8. **Q-45:** nothing covers an outage of Cloudflare itself, so `bookingPausedError` exists only while the Workers run.
9. **New, the kind of each limit's outcome:** arch 06 lines 299 to 309 give each limit's actions; 02-contracts K-09 gives the three kinds; the pairing in the table above is this contract's.
10. **New, one commit per turn:** the planner's verbs and every shared-core call made in a turn ride inside `gateAndCommit` (arch 02 line 121); a call made alone commits once under `caller.requestId`.
11. **New, the fallback is code's:** `finalizeMessage` fills the step's fallback text itself after a failed rewrite (arch 02 line 123), so one call returns at most two check results.
12. **New, proof codes:** the code goes to the email on file, or the person texts it from the mobile it proves, since Rosie never texts first (MC C9); on an email route the code never comes back to the caller.
13. **NQ-RT-4:** a clock's firing is a turn on the handle's actor (or a sweep job) whose only input is the clock row; it calls `gateAndCommit` with `input.kind` clock and no decide reads, and commits through K-05 v1 `fireClock`.

## Defaults used (T-018)

14. **New, one setup call per step:** the setup writes of journeys 1 and 2 are `saveSetupStep`, one request variant per step, with the keys written in `gives` (arch 12 line 590: the Tool API generated from the needs and gives keys); in a turn the write is the chosen choice's effect and `partResult.gives` names its keys, including those of the automatic steps its walk passes.
15. **New, exchanges:** every fixture is a `<call>.exchange`, as K-05's; an exchange with `caller.kind` turn of any call but `gateAndCommit` shows the action as it rides inside the turn's commit.
16. **Q-02, control proof:** `sendProofCode` with purpose control and route listingEmail (no handle), and `proveCode`'s `controlBusinessId`; with no listing email, `openReviewItem` kind control (1 business day).
17. **CQ-46, S1-77:** K-29 v1's chat links have no purpose for a link that is just for a person, so v1 records the helper's answer (S1-09 `helper`) and S1-77's link waits for K-29's minor version and the K-09 minor version that adopts it; until then the named owner joins by texting Rosie first (S1-01; MC C9).
18. **New, keys in unfrozen parts:** a write to K-22, K-25, K-27 or K-35 is named in words, and `written` lists only rows of frozen parts; the fixtures say so where it applies.
19. **New, S1-32:** under MC C10 the ID upload counts as confirmed, so the go-live check reads the upload and control and returns the master's branch label; no ID-check service is called.
20. **New, `matchKinds`' budget:** none in the sources; proposed at most 200 ms, as default 3.
21. **New, placeholders:** Rosie's number and the link form are config values (Q-51), and the staff join's ready-text words are not in the sources (C-103 names only "a staff join code"); the fixtures use placeholders.
22. **New, the staff invite's reminder:** a staff link arms a 48-hour clock (S2-05, S2-07; K-29 chat_links.owner_retold_at), returned as `ownerRetoldClockRowId`.
23. **New, `emailCheck`:** FS row B13's email code at sign-up is `sendProofCode` purpose emailCheck.

## Defaults used (T-018, round 2)

24. **NQ-RT-4, the clock turn:** a clock's firing on a row a handle owns is a turn on that handle's actor, `caller.kind` turn; its one event id is the clock row's id (K-05 v1 default 9), its id that event id's hash (CQ-09), its trace id the firing's own (CQ-06). A firing with no turn (`turn` null) is `caller.kind` clockFiring, keyed by the clock row. Its answer is `fired`, as K-05 v1 `fireClock`'s.
25. **Q-41, limit 6 before the re-read:** while `decide.reread` is false, a value that fails its words is refused before any write (`valueFailsWords`), the turn's core actions included, and the turn comes back to the gate after C-116's one Sonnet re-read; only parts the gate checks count, so a danger text's held parts never wait for a re-read and its checked line is not delayed.
26. **New, limit 1's code:** the code limit 1 sends is typed back in the sender's own chat, as the answer to an open question the gate opens there in the same commit (S13-45: typed back in the same conversation); `proveCode` then names the waiting turn (`redecideTurnId`), whose choice is decided again from today's record, as a released safety hold's is.
27. **New, the owner's email (FS row B13):** `sendProofCode` route typedEmail writes the typed address's K-04 handle when it is new (the code's email row needs it as its recipient), keeps the address as the owner email of `forPersonId`'s account, not yet proven (K-22 v1 `accounts.email`, written on the runtime side), and sends the code there; the call acts for no person until the code is right, so `caller.personId` is null, and `forPersonId`, the person the secure page's own signed link was made for, names whose account it is, set by the page's server side, never by the browser (K-22 v1 rule 4; default 34). `proveCode` then marks it proven (K-22 v1 `accounts.email_proven_at`) and links the handle to that person (K-04 person_handles); nothing uses it before (S1-10 lines 172 to 173).
28. **New, review write-backs:** a done decision carries a typed write-back that the action layer applies to the row under review in the same commit, never read from the outcome's words (arch 02 line 139). S1-76's listing_match writes control as S1-75's control item does (K-23 v1's control columns cite both steps); the kinds of journeys 3, 11 and 12 write back through their own minor versions. For QUESTIONS.md: whether S1-76's check proves control.
29. **New, STOP and START bound to the number (RULES 7.2, 7.5; MC C1):** START counts only from the phone's own START text, named by its event, in that text's turn or written on return by its actor; the schema refuses any other caller. A STOP from a page, by email or by a call stops only the caller's own mobile; anything else answers `notFromThatPhone`.
30. **New, the ready text's code (security review N1):** `sendProofCode.response.readyText` holds the code, so, like an email row's final parts, it never enters a span, a trace or the event log.
31. **New, a hold's "we're safe" (security review N4):** `releaseSafetyHold`'s `releasedByOutcomeId` must be the safety read of a text from the held handle's own chat (A3-24, A3-25); another handle's answers `notAllowed`.
32. **New, what the AI layer sees (AI review notes c, d):** C-114 leaves `context.writer.handle`, the writer's own number, out of the prompt; for the one rewrite C-118 sends the model only each failed check and its detail, never the filled final parts, and C-092's details name slots, never filled values.
33. **K-08 v1 default 3 in `written`:** a phone row is named by its handle id, never its number, as the fixtures do, although K-05 v1's `rowRef.rowId` is the primary key and K-04 keys phones by number (open gap 22).

## Defaults used (T-018, round 3)

34. **K-22 v1, the person a code is for:** `sendProofCode.forPersonId` is K-22 v1 `proof_codes.person_id`: an id for every purpose but control, set by the server (from the secure page's own signed link for the owner's email, K-21; from the account the typed contact finds for a sign-in; by the gate for limit 1), never by the browser. On a sign-in's and the owner email's send `caller.personId` is null, so `rosie.person_id` stays unset until the code is right (K-22 v1 rule 4). Each K-09 purpose is one K-22 v1 purpose ("Proof codes in K-22 v1"): limit 1's `unprovenSender` is `link_record`, since its code proves the sender's own handle as that person's (RULES 6.1, 6.5; S13-45: "Before a phone links to an old record"); and the control and typed-email routes write the email's K-04 handle when it is new, since K-22 v1's `sent_to_handle_id` is not null.
35. **New, a bare STOP in its own turn (action-layer note N3; security note N7):** the handle actor runs a text that is only an opt-out word (arch 02 line 115) in its own core-only turn as soon as it arrives, never joined to a turn of decided texts nor restarting one that waits for C-116's re-read (arch 02 line 113's restart), so `valueFailsWords` never holds a STOP back (RULES 7.2: a stop works at once; arch 06 line 329: changes that only protect commit at once).

## Open gaps

1. **K-08 kinds for the setup writes (CQ-56):** K-08 v1.1 has no kind for picked kinds, plug-in texts, new work drafts (a review's decision written back included), companies, company kinds, the business setup row and its photos (control proven by the team included), places or things, rules, price OK limits, bill cards, a chat link used or its owner told again (S2-05, S2-07), the person_handles row `proveCode` adds for a typed email, nor for K-22's, K-25's, K-27's and K-35's rows, nor a calendar link dropped or back. CQ-56's default: K-08 adds each kind with the version that first writes it, a minor version before the first journey 1 task that writes them (F14). For QUESTIONS.md under CQ-56.
2. **Parts not adopted yet:** K-22 v1 (T-031), K-25 v1 (T-034) and K-28 v1 (T-037) froze on main after this contract was first written, and this branch is rebased on them; K-27, K-35 and the ports K-12 to K-16 are not frozen. v1 maps K-22 v1's proof codes and the call's person (`rosie.person_id`) as frozen ("Proof codes in K-22 v1"). The rest is named in words: K-22's staff rows, the owner's ID upload record, the emergency contact, sessions, on call and the staff page; K-25's services, service money and staff prices; K-27's calendar links, busy blocks and class sessions (`calendarLinkDropped`, `calendarLinkBack`); K-28's customers (journey 3); K-35's licenses and agreements. The K-09 minor version that adopts each part states its columns: K-22, K-25, K-27 and K-35 with the first journey 1 or 2 task that writes them (after F14), K-28 with v1.1 (journey 3).
3. **CQ-44 (a write with no turn):** a page's, the console's or a job's call commits under `caller.requestId`, which needs a K-05 call that commits with no turn; such writes wait for it, and in a turn they ride in `gateAndCommit`.
4. **CQ-46 (S1-77):** default 17.
5. **K-05 v1.2 (T-072):** the guard reads a handle's targets through K-05 v1.2's state reads; `buildFrontier` takes them as given (`guardTargets`).
6. **The planner's protecting change** (arch 06 line 291): K-02 v1's five planner verbs hold none, so a protecting change reaches the gate through decide's `enter` on its any-time row.
7. **CQ-32:** how a paged person's reply reaches a page: here, `acknowledgePage` as a core action of that person's turn, or a console tap.
8. **Fixed lines:** the outage lines, the stop, start and HELP replies and the safety lines are K-01 fixed lines by id; their words wait on Q-01 and Jay's review.
9. **Rosie's number and the link host** in `issueChatLink` are config values (Q-51).
10. **`bookingPausedError.retryAfterSeconds`:** no source gives it; the runtime sets it.
11. **Time budgets:** only `buildFrontier` (150 ms) and `gateAndCommit` (300 ms) have one in the sources; the rest are open (defaults 3 and 20).
12. **No fixture for S1-01's thread, S1-21's rules or S1-81's spaces:** the rule ids wait for K-26's registry seeds, and the sample cast's demo shop books no space; `STEP_GIVES` and the variants state them.
13. **The kind ids in the fixtures** (`barbershop`, `plumbing`, `electrical`) illustrate K-24's id form; the 243 kinds' ids are K-24's seeds.

From the first verification's notes (round 2), each one a later minor version can add, or another contract's or a decision's. For QUESTIONS.md:

14. **The planner's refusal reason** (AI note b): when code refuses the planner's output, `gateAndCommit` answers `planner` null and its parts take the passed-on path; the reason (`factNotInContext`, `moreThanOneQuestion`) is not returned. A K-09 minor version adds an optional field.
15. **The composer input of planner and one-question rows** (AI note a): the planner's answer, question and pass-on rows (`plannerVerbs.response.outboxRowIds`) and the one-question rows reach the composer through `composeInput`, but K-03 v1's input has no field for the planner's words (plannerAnswer.text, plannerAsk.question), what is unclear (ask.unclear) or a pass-on this turn committed (arch 07 line 403), and `masterText.step` needs a K-01 step id. A K-03 minor version and one K-09 line, before T-371 (M3b).
16. **Fixed-line rows and the composer** (security note N5): while PostgreSQL is up the safety, STOP, START and HELP replies are outbox rows (NQ-RT-9; default 6); nothing yet says those fixed-line rows skip `composeInput` and how their plan is written. A K-03 or K-09 minor version.
17. **A sign-in code by the text route** (security note N1): `textFromMobile`'s ready text goes back to whoever started the sign-in, who then needs only the owner to text it; texting the code into an open chat instead would avoid that (a new route, by a minor version, or a decision for the security reviewer and the CTO).
18. **STOP's notice to the businesses** (security note N2): RULES 7.5's "Texts to this customer are off. Reach them their way." to each business with an open booking for that phone: journey 3's minor version (v1.1) adds it.
19. **Limits on a call made alone** (security note N3): `saveSetupStep.errors` has no error carrying a failed limit, so a page's or the console's call relies on the call itself being the yes; a minor version adds one before the M4 page tasks.
20. **Fixtures still to come** (QA and eval note 2; frontend notes 1, 2 and 4): the error and repeat branches (`waitingForContact`, `indexUnavailable`, a wrong code and its pause, `transient`, `repeated`, the clock's `notArmed` and `notDue`), `acknowledgePage`, `handOff`, `answerHandOff` and a review item set waiting; K-21's console fixtures carry each row's id and version, and K-21 says how a page gets the event ids it sends (`yesEventId`, `provenByEventId`). Minor versions and K-21 add them.
21. **The limit fixtures' seeds** (action-layer note e): `given` lists no seeds; the state each needs is in its note, and limit 4's changed row is K-35's (not frozen). QA's T-088 builds them.
22. **K-05 and phone rows** (action-layer note c): K-05 v1's `rowRef.rowId` is the primary key and K-04 keys phones by number, while K-08 v1 default 3 and these fixtures name a phone row by its handle id (default 33); and `written` is K-05 v1's `commitResult`, without K-05 v1.1's `waitingChat`. K-05's next minor version says which.
23. **Telling the owner a review's result** (S1-03: "Sam hears the result within 1 business day"; S1-75): a message from a call made alone, which waits for CQ-44 (open gap 3).
24. **Sessions and the code purposes v1 leaves out** (action-layer note b): v1 maps its proof codes, the gate's limit 1 code included, to K-22 v1 as frozen (`signIn` `sign_in`, `emailCheck` `owner_email`, `unprovenSender` `link_record`, `newContact` `contact_change`, `control` `listing_control`: "Proof codes in K-22 v1"). Still open: the session a sign-in starts (K-22 v1 `sessions`, with K-21's sign-in), and the second proof a sign-in on a new device needs before it (S13-45 row 8: a `new_device` code to the owner's email, or the ID re-check; `proof-code-text-from-mobile.json`'s new computer), which the K-09 minor version that adopts K-22's sessions adds; `closing_or_export` (RULES 6.9) and `hand_over` (S11-100), with journeys 11 and 13's minor versions; and how K-05 keeps a code's id and person on the server between its send and its check (K-22 v1 open gaps: a K-05 minor version).
25. **Optional fields for the setup parts** (action-layer note f): S1-12's price kind, S1-13's places, per-place prices, deposit and fee, and S2-15's prices (K-25); S1-84's FAQs (K-23 business_faqs) and a photo's written yes; a K-03 slot for a proof code; a K-08 kind for S2-02's new phone row; the agent token on `caller` (K-20, C-110). Each a minor version's optional field.
26. **A turnless clock fixture:** no clock row of journeys 1 and 2 is owned by no handle and writes no outbox row, so the turnless shape is shown by the schema and the rule `clockTurn` only; the first such row (a saved time's release) comes with journey 4.

## v1.1 (T-050): journey 3

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): 02-contracts K-09's version table, row v1.1 ("the ways in: an AI's request, the booking page, a booking the business adds (read back with 'did they agree to messages?', and the chat link and QR for the owner to pass on: C9), a walk-in; what it is about: new, a booking already made (the page link first and nothing said before the code check: A3-43), someone else, a person asked for (a message taken), a wrong number, a child; find or add the customer by mobile; block a customer (S11-62)"), frozen before journey 3's first task (section 9, F15); T-246's open point: K-09 v1 names no caller and no first-version rule for the handle actor's safety record on arrival (`ops/cards/T-246.md`, defaults); v1's open gap 18 (STOP's notice to the businesses, assigned to v1.1); and the first verification's points on round 1 (`ops/cards/T-050.md`, round 2 below). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `tool-api.v1.1.schema.json` holds every v1 definition under its own name: the unchanged ones (148) by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.schema.json`, which stays as it is; the changed ones (`given`, `partResult`, `coreResult`, `gateAndCommit.committed`, `gateAndCommit.response`, `gateAndCommit.exchange`, `recordSafetyOutcome.request`, `recordSafetyOutcome.response`, `recordSafetyOutcome.exchange`, `releaseSafetyHold.request`, `releaseSafetyHold.exchange`, `recordStopOrStart.response`, `recordStopOrStart.exchange`, `callName`) take only new optional fields, new enum values or new request variants. So every v1 exchange is a v1.1 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every v1 fixture against v1.1), and a v1.1 call always returns the new fields, with one exception (round 2: action-layer note f): `recordSafetyOutcome` made as a core action of a turn answers in `gateAndCommit.response.core`, whose result for every core action but a STOP's is v1's `coreResult` unchanged, so there it carries no `gives` and no `hold.version`. The outcome row it writes is the key safe all the same, and a hold it writes is at version 1 all the same (K-34 v1 rule 2; default 37); only the at-once release on arrival names that version, and the arrival record, made alone, returns it. **Scope:** journey 3's steps A3-01 to A3-51 and B3-01 to B3-42, the call-only steps out (C-229, C-231, C-232: A3-05 to A3-09, A3-11, A3-42, A3-14, A3-13, A3-31, A3-15, A3-27, A3-17; B3-03, B3-10 to B3-12, B3-16, B3-26, B3-34, B3-42, B3-22, B3-25, B3-04 to B3-06), and S11-62 with journey 3 (BP starting shape, M5 row). K-28 v1 (customers) and K-30 v1 (bookings and changes), frozen on main, are named by table and column, never redefined.

### The calls v1.1 adds

Every call reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached** (arch 02 line 117; arch 10 lines 504, 538) and can also answer `transient`. Every journey 3 call runs under one business: `$defs/businessCaller` is v1's caller with `businessId` never null, and row-level security scopes every row it touches (arch 10 line 546; K-28 v1 and K-30 v1 rules 4 and 5). A journey 3 step's write in a turn is the chosen choice's effect inside `gateAndCommit`, checked by limits 1 to 6, and its committed part names the keys (`partResult.gives`, which v1.1 lets name journey 3's keys); an exchange of the call with `caller.kind` turn shows that write as it rides there.

| Call                      | Gives (steps)                                                                                                                                                                | Callers (schema-held)                                                                                                                                                            | Person                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Repeating it                                                                                                                                    | Version rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Errors                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
