| `openRequest`             | request (A3-01: A3-02, A3-04, A3-19, A3-20's door; B3-01: B3-02, B3-07, B3-08's BOOK); at door business it also stores the values the BOOK names, giving no key (default 43) | door ai: agent only; door page: page only; door chat: turn only; door business: turn or page; door walk_in: turn or page, flow A. ai and page are read first (`safetyOutcomeId`) | The booker is the caller's own person (set by the server: the handle's, K-21's session, K-20's token), a customer on the business's own list (`customerId`), or a new person the call writes; never a person id the browser or the AI sends                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Safe: `caller.requestId` (a turn's id in a turn) is the key; a repeat writes nothing and answers the first request (`repeated`)                 | Inserts only: the K-04 head and the K-30 details row at version 1 (K-04 v1 rule 2; K-30 v1 rule 4), at door business with `taken` 'nothing' (default 55); at door business also the K-30 line, a new customer's K-04 handles, and the time held by K-05 reserve, each at version 1 | `notLive`, `safetyHeld`, `waitsForOwner`, `customerBlocked` (no block named, no reason), `notAllowed`, `notFound`, `bookingPaused`, `constraint` |
| `confirmBusinessBooking`  | who_for, reach_by, times, customer (A3-30, B3-08; '2 Not yet' is A3-40, B3-24), and books it as entered on either answer; '3 Change it' gives none                           | turn (the reply in the answerer's own chat) or page (the Today page); never an agent, a job or the console                                                                       | `caller.personId` is the owner, an admin or staff of the business who answered (else `notAllowed`); the customer is the head's booker, which the request does not name; a customer already on the list keeps the contacts and the way on file, taken from the row (another phone or email waits for their own code, S13-45 row 3; a way changes on their own word, A7-98), so the request carries no contact (default 49); the read-back's words and versions come from its open question in a turn, or from `readBackBusinessBooking` through the page server, never the browser | Safe: `caller.requestId` is the key; a repeat answers the first result                                                                          | The head's version compared and set (`expectVersion`, limit 4), and every row the read-back was built from compared; the held reservation confirmed booked at its version; a listed customer raised only when an age band it lacked is filled; a new customer row at version 1     | `notAllowed`, `wrongDoor`, `customerBlocked` (its own block named), `timeTaken`, `notFound`, `versionChanged`, `constraint`, `bookingPaused`     |
| `readBackBusinessBooking` | none: the Today page's read-back of A3-30, B3-08 (default 52)                                                                                                                | page only (the Today page's server side)                                                                                                                                         | The owner, an admin or staff of the business, set by the page server from its session (else `notAllowed`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Safe: a read; the same versions give the same words                                                                                             | Reads and returns the version of every row the words were built from                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `notAllowed`, `notFound`, `wrongDoor`, `bookingPaused`                                                                                           |
| `findCustomer`            | none: a read for the read-back (the plan's "find or add the customer by mobile"; the add is the call above's)                                                                | turn or page                                                                                                                                                                     | The owner, an admin or staff of the business (else `notAllowed`); returns only this business's records, never a number or a booking                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Safe: a read                                                                                                                                    | Reads the version of each row it returns                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `notAllowed`, `bookingPaused`                                                                                                                    |
| `checkBookingFirst`       | none (A3-43, B3-35: the page's tap)                                                                                                                                          | page only                                                                                                                                                                        | `caller.personId` null: the tap acts for no one until the code is right (K-22 v1 rule 4); the code's person stays on the server with the attempt id                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Safe: `caller.requestId` is the key; no second code, the same attempt; a contact on file gets at most one live code (default 51)                | Writes a K-22 code row only when a booking matches, version 1; the answer is the same either way                                                                                                                                                                                   | `bookingPaused` (none tells whether a booking exists)                                                                                            |
| `setCustomerBlock`        | none (S11-62)                                                                                                                                                                | turn (the owner tells the AI, read back first) or page (the Customers page)                                                                                                      | The account owner or an admin he lets (else `notAllowed`); the person blocked is found from a customer row or a threat's safety read of this business, never sent by the page                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Safe: an open block of that number at this business, or a lifted block, answers `repeated`; an address is compared with the open ones, unsealed | A block is inserted at version 1; a lift compares and sets the block's version (`expectVersion`)                                                                                                                                                                                   | `notAllowed`, `notFound`, `versionChanged`, `constraint` (a number not that person's own), `bookingPaused`                                       |

No source gives a time budget for these calls; in a turn each runs inside `gateAndCommit`'s 300 ms (arch 04 line 223), and alone none has one (open gap 11).

**What each writes** (the schema's descriptions say it in full, with sources):

- **`openRequest`** writes, in one commit, the K-04 booking head (stage `entry`, the flow, the booker; person served and payer null until who_for) and K-30 v1 `booking_details` (`door`, `made_by` from the door, `booking_details_made_by_door`, the booking `number`, and at door business `taken` 'nothing', set once in that insert: default 55), and a new K-04 person when the booker is new. It is written once the door's words read as a new booking (A3-51, B3-40), after their danger check (default 42). Before any write it checks that the business is live (`notLive`: B3-01 needs live), that the request's own read holds no danger and no open hold blocks booking (`safetyHeld`: arch 02 line 129), that no pause holds the person or phone (`waitsForOwner`: A3-48) and that no open block holds a known person or number (`customerBlocked`, with no reason: S11-62). At door business it takes the values the BOOK words or the Today page entry name (`entered`: decide's part fields in that turn, each re-parsed from the BOOK words with that text's send time under limit 6, or the page's fields) and stores each where the read-back and the reply read it as a fact with its version (default 43): the service as the K-30 `booking_lines` row written on the owner's BOOK (K-30 README), a new customer's contacts as their own K-04 handles (a phone row and a contacts/add effect for a mobile: Q-03), both in the same commit, and the time entered held for the booking by K-05 v1 `reserve` right after that commit (the head must exist first: K-04 `reservations.booking_id`), before the read-back is composed. A time already taken leaves `held` null, and the read-back offers the next open times (limit 5): the open times of the resource entered after the time entered, which C-118 gets as facts with versions from the K-05 read open gap 44 names (round 2: AI note d); until that read exists the read-back says the time entered is taken and offers no other, and '3 Change it' takes a new one. These writes give no key: who_for, reach_by, times and customer stay the reply's.
- **`confirmBusinessBooking`** confirms what the BOOK stored, never a value from the read-back's words, and books it as entered on '1 Yes' and '2 Not yet' alike (A3-30: "The reply books it, as entered", MASTER-FLOW-A line 1956, B line 1746; A4-98; A4-14's and A5-31's vary; A3-40 "The booking is made"; K-30 README: the commit that writes the booking includes A3-30): the held reservation confirmed `booked` with the booking through K-05 v1 commitTurn (its saved-time clock row cancelled), and the head's stage `booked`, so the 10-minute sweep never lets the time go. In the same commit it writes customer (the K-28 row found by business and booker, or added with the booker's name and the contacts the BOOK stored as their own K-04 handles, and the K-04 `person_roles` customer row: K-28 v1 rule 7), reach_by (`customers.way`: the one on file, or for a new customer the way their contacts imply), who_for (the head's person served and payer, the customer it is for, and `customers.age_band`: the one on file, or 18 or older as the read-back said; open gap 41) and times (`booking_details.times`, set once, from the business's flow default: K-23 `flow_defaults.arrive`). For '1 Yes, send the link' also the customer's yes to messages for this booking, a K-29 v1 `consents` row of kind `texts` bound to the read-back's exact words and their sha256 (RULES 2.5; K-28 v1 default 5), after which the owner gets the customer's chat link and QR to pass on (`issueChatLink`, purpose `booking`, `given_via` owner, once contacts/add has worked: MC C9; Q-03; C-254); for a stopped mobile instead no consent and no link, and the one who answered hears only "Texts to this customer are off. Reach them their way." (`textsOff`; default 50): in a turn as an outbox row in their own chat, and on the Today page, whose commit writes no outbox row (open gap 43), shown by the page itself, with `textsOff.outboxRowId` null (`confirmBusinessBooking.exchange` holds the row to the caller: `j3-confirm-business-booking-texts-off-page.json`). '2 Not yet' writes no consent and no link, and the business tells the customer the details (A3-40, B3-24; default 44). '3 Change it' (`changeIt`) takes the changed values in the turn or page call that holds them, re-parsed there under limit 6, stores them as the BOOK's were (a new hold, a new line, or a BOOK-added customer's corrected name and contacts), gives no key and books nothing; the booking is then read back again (default 53).
- **`readBackBusinessBooking`** (the Today page only) answers the read-back's exact words, their sha256 and the rows and versions they were built from; the words are code's, A3-30's read-back line with its slots filled from the stored facts, so the same versions give the same words (default 52). The page server keeps them with its session and sends them back with the confirm tap; the call refills them from those versions and refuses words that differ. In a turn the read-back is the BOOK turn's own reply row (C-118), kept with its open question, and the reply names that open question.
- **`recordStopOrStart`** (v1's call, its response extended): a STOP now also tells each business with an open booking for that phone, once and in the same commit, only "Texts to this customer are off. Reach them their way." (`businessesTold`; default 50; v1's open gap 18). A STOP made with no turn (a page's or an email's for the caller's own number, or one its actor writes on return) cannot write an outbox row, so its commit arms one K-04 clock row per such business, due at once, whose clock turn on the owner's handle writes the notice within the minute; `businessesTold` names each business with `outboxRowId` null (default 56; open gap 43; `j3-stop-on-return-tells-businesses.json`).
- **`findCustomer`** reads the business's own customer rows by mobile, or by the name the BOOK words give, with each row's version, and never says anything about a booking (A3-41).
- **`checkBookingFirst`**: if one booking at this business matches the name and the day typed, a K-22 `link_record` code goes to its contact on file, their way (default 45); the answer is `sentIfMatched` either way, and proveCode (v1) takes the code back. A contact on file gets at most one live code from this tap: while one lives, or its 15-minute pause holds, a new tap sends nothing and answers the same (default 51).
- **`setCustomerBlock`** writes or lifts a K-28 v1 `customer_blocks` row (number, or the address for home visits sealed under the blocked person's key) and lists the bookings already made with that person, which the owner keeps or cancels each with no fee (journey 7's cancel, v1.5). A block adds no customer row or role (K-28 v1 default 11).

### Each key's one call (02-contracts section 8)

| Key      | Given by (A-J3.md, B-J3.md)       | Its home (02-contracts section 8)                         | The one call             |
| -------- | --------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------ |
| request  | A3-01, A3-02, A3-04, A3-19; B3-01 | K-30: a booking request in progress (`booking_details`)   | `openRequest`            |
| safe     | A3-22; B3-17                      | K-34: the turn's safety outcome (`safety_outcomes`)       | `recordSafetyOutcome`    |
| who_for  | A3-30; B3-08                      | K-04 booking head (booker, person served, payer) and K-30 | `confirmBusinessBooking` |
| reach_by | A3-30; B3-08                      | K-28: the customer's way (`customers.way`)                | `confirmBusinessBooking` |
| times    | A3-30; B3-08                      | K-30: the times card shown and the time chosen            | `confirmBusinessBooking` |
| customer | A3-30; B3-08                      | K-28: the customer row                                    | `confirmBusinessBooking` |

A3-06 and A3-14 also give request, and are out (C-231). What `openRequest` stores at the BOOK (the line, a new customer's K-04 handles, the time held: default 43) gives no key: those are the values the reply confirms, and the four keys stay `confirmBusinessBooking`'s. `readBackBusinessBooking`, `findCustomer`, `checkBookingFirst` and `setCustomerBlock` give none. Each call's request description names its keys with their step ids, and `recordSafetyOutcome` and the two new calls answer the keys they wrote in `gives`. The named rule `givesJourneyKeys` checks every fixture that states a journey 3 step: it writes exactly that step's keys, and, made alone, by the key's one call (`tool-api.rules.mjs`, `J3_STEP_GIVES` and `KEY_CALL`). Journey 4's steps that give the same keys (A4-14, A4-23, A4-80, A4-82 and twins) are K-09 v1.2's.

### The safety record on arrival (T-246's open point)

T-246 records a text's safety outcome before any turn, through `recordSafetyOutcome` with `caller.kind` job, `turnId` null and `requestId` `safety:<eventId>`, and releases an at-once hold with `expectVersion` 1, because v1 names no caller and no first-version rule for that early record. v1's answer (default 5: the outcome rides in the turn its text joins, and with danger the actor closes the batch at once) does not cover it, and the job caller cannot carry it: K-34 v1 holds a text's outcome to a turn (`safety_outcomes_text_has_handle_and_turn`), and K-04 an outbox row to its turn (`outbox_rows.turn_id` not null), so a record with no turn is refused by the database. v1's request stays valid in v1.1 (a minor version only adds), so the action layer answers a text's outcome (source text) sent alone with no turn with `constraint` (`safety_outcomes_text_has_handle_and_turn`, 23514) and writes nothing; v1's page and agent records, which carry no turn, are unchanged. v1.1's answer, only adding:

- **`caller.kind` actorOnArrival** (`$defs/arrivalCaller`; default 36): the handle actor records a text whose read holds danger or timed out at once, before batching, in the text's own **arrival turn**: one event id, the text's; its id the hash of a marked input, `arrivalTurnId(eventId)`: the SHA-256 of the UTF-8 bytes of `arrival:` followed by the event id, in lowercase hex (`tool-api.rules.mjs` exports the function, so the actor and QA compute it the same way), as a clock turn's input is its clock row's id (K-05 v1 default 9); its trace id the text's own (CQ-06). `recordSafetyOutcome.arrival` carries that turn (K-05 v1 `turn`), and the call commits through K-05 v1 commitTurn: the outcome row, its hold, the checked line as an outbox row of send class safety and who is told, in one transaction. The text joins its batch's turn too, and **the two ids never meet**: a batch turn's id is the hash of its sorted event ids alone (CQ-09; T-248 builds it), never the marked input, so even the usual batch of the danger text alone (default 5 closes it at once) gets an id of its own, and K-05 v1 commitTurn writes it instead of answering `repeated` with nothing written (K-05 README item 1). Its `recordSafetyOutcome` core action for the text answers `repeated` (the event id is the key), its routine parts the hold answers `held` while it is open, and a STOP in any words or a protecting change in that text commits there at once, past the hold (RULES 7.2; MASTER-FLOW-A line 7208; default 35): `j3-safety-arrival-batch-turn.json`, after `j3-safety-arrival-emergency.json` (whose words hold neither, so its commit is the turn and the safety record's repeat), and `j3-safety-arrival-threat-batch.json`, after `j3-safety-arrival-threat.json` (A3-48: its commit writes the pause and the visit's call-off clock). The rule `arrivalTurn` checks the arrival id from the event id, and that a batch turn's id is none of its texts' arrival ids.
- **A read that timed out** (no verdict in 4 s: arch 02 line 111) is recorded on arrival too: with a danger word, that word's safety line, an outbox row of send class safety (`j3-safety-arrival-timeout.json`, K-02's `safety-timeout-danger-word.json` word for word); with none, the outage line, an outbox row of send class quick_reply as T-246 sends it, named by `safetyLine` the same way (its fixed line id and row); routine is held either way. K-02 v1 has no fixture of a timeout with no danger word, so that branch has none here. A read of No has no arrival record: it rides in the turn its text joins, as default 5 says (`invalid/refuse-j3-arrival-record-of-no.json`). Only these two variants take the arrival caller, and only alone, never inside `gateAndCommit`.
- **The first version rule** (default 37): a hold the call writes is at version 1 (K-34 v1 rule 2: `version integer not null default 1`), and v1.1's answer returns it (`hold.version`, its stored version on a repeat). The actor's at-once release (`releaseSafetyHold.arrival`) names it (`expectVersion`) under the same arrival turn, with `why` `outcome_message_sent` only, once it has handed the checked line on (arch 06 line 319).
- **For T-246's code** (a new task for the runtime engineer, outside this card): the early record moves from the job caller to `actorOnArrival` with the arrival turn, and the release takes its version from `hold.version`.

### Named rules added (`tool-api.rules.mjs`)

v1's four rules stay as they are; v1.1 adds two under new names.

- **`givesJourneyKeys`:** an exchange whose `given.stated` names a journey 3 step writes exactly the keys that step gives (`J3_STEP_GIVES`, from A-J3.md and B-J3.md), its outcome wrote exactly the stated keys, and, made alone, its call is the one call that writes each key (`KEY_CALL`).
- **`arrivalTurn`:** a call made by `actorOnArrival` names its arrival turn as `caller.turnId` and `requestId`, a SHA-256 in hex; a record's turn id is `arrivalTurnId` of its text's event id, its one event id the text's, its handle and its trace id the text's, its read holds danger or timed out, its answer gives safe, and a hold it wrote is at version 1. On the batch turn after an arrival record (`gateAndCommit`), its id is none of its texts' arrival ids, it commits under its own id (`repeated` false), and each text's safety record in it answers `repeated`. `ARRIVAL_MARK` and `arrivalTurnId` are exported beside the rule.

### Producers and consumers of v1.1

- **Producer:** action-layer engineer: C-074 Tool API, with C-099 bookings and quotes (`openRequest`, `confirmBusinessBooking`, `readBackBusinessBooking`), C-100 reservations (the BOOK's hold, the reply's booking), C-094 identity and access (`findCustomer`, `checkBookingFirst`, a new customer's handles), C-105 block and pause (`setCustomerBlock`), C-104 safety and hand-offs (the arrival record and release; the shared core's STOP and its `businessesTold`), C-087 one commit per turn, as journey 3's tasks land.
- **Consumers:** runtime engineer: C-046 handle actor and C-047 safety read on arrival (the arrival record with `arrivalTurnId`, the batch turn's own id, the release; T-246's rework); action-layer engineer: C-110 Agent API (door ai; K-20, T-029, which freezes after this version); frontend engineer, through K-21's journey 3 version (T-060): C-197 booking page (door page), C-192 owner console (the Today page's entry, read-back and confirm tap; the Customers page's block), C-199 secure page (the A3-43 tap); AI engineer: C-114 context bundle and C-115, C-116 decide (A3-19's values as fields in the BOOK turn, A3-51's and A3-30's choices), C-118 composer (the read-back's slots from the stored facts); architect: C-186 Tool API mock (it answers v1.1's exchanges too); QA: the contract test `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.1.test.ts` (T-117), C-140 eval harness, C-147 scenario tests.
- **Frozen before it, read here:** K-09 v1 (T-018), K-28 v1 (T-037), K-30 v1 (T-039), with v1's own (K-01 to K-05, K-07, K-08, K-22 for proof codes, K-23, K-24, K-26, K-29, K-34, K-36, K-37).
- **Not here:** QA's contract test `tool-api.v1.1.test.ts` (T-117); journey 3's K-21 screens (T-060); K-20 itself (T-029); building the calls (the action layer's journey 3 tasks).

### Fixtures, v1.1 (in `fixtures/index.json` with version v1.1)

28 accepted and 10 refused, each refused for its one fault (with that fault fixed it is accepted: checked 2026-11-07, round 2; round 3 added three accepted fixtures).

- **The ways in:** `j3-safety-agent-request.json` and `j3-open-request-agent.json` (A3-02: read first, then door ai for a new nameless person), `j3-safety-page-request.json` and `j3-open-request-page.json` (B3-07, B3-01: door page, flow B), `j3-open-request-business-text.json` (A3-19: Sam's BOOK for Alex on the list, in his turn, its values stored: the line, the time held), `j3-open-request-business-desk.json` (the Today page: Tessa, new, flow B, with her mobile as her own handle), `j3-open-request-walk-in.json` (A3-20: Omar's own text), `j3-open-request-blocked.json` (S11-62: Kim's text while blocked, `customerBlocked` with no reason).
- **The read-back:** `j3-find-customer.json` (by mobile, the barbershop's record only), `j3-read-back-page.json` (the Today page's words and versions from `readBackBusinessBooking`), `j3-confirm-business-booking-yes.json` (A3-30: '1 Yes' names the open question; booked as entered, the four keys and the texts consent), `j3-confirm-business-booking-not-yet.json` (B3-08, B3-24: '2 Not yet' on the page with the server's words; booked as entered, the four keys, no consent), `j3-confirm-business-booking-not-yet-text.json` (A3-30 then A3-40 by text: '2 Not yet' is on the frontier because the BOOK wrote `taken` 'nothing'; booked as entered, the four keys, no consent), `j3-confirm-business-booking-texts-off.json` ('1 Yes' for a stopped mobile: booked, no consent, only the texts-off line), `j3-confirm-business-booking-texts-off-page.json` (the same on the Today page: the line has no outbox row and the page shows it), `j3-confirm-business-booking-change.json` ('3 Change it': the new time held, nothing booked, no key).
- **A booking already made:** `j3-check-booking-first.json` (A3-43: the same answer whether or not one matches).
- **Safety on arrival:** `j3-safety-arrival-emergency.json` (A3-24), `j3-safety-arrival-batch-turn.json` (the lone-text batch after it, under its own id: it commits), `j3-safety-arrival-threat.json` and `j3-safety-arrival-threat-batch.json` (A3-48: the threat's record, then its lone-text batch, whose commit writes the protecting change the danger text calls for, the pause and its call-off clock), `j3-safety-arrival-own-line.json` (A3-26, at once), `j3-safety-arrival-release.json` (its release, version 1) and `j3-safety-arrival-timeout.json` (a read that timed out with a danger word).
- **STOP:** `j3-stop-tells-businesses.json` (Alex's STOP: the barbershop with his open booking hears only "Texts to this customer are off. Reach them their way.") and `j3-stop-on-return-tells-businesses.json` (the same STOP written on return with no turn: a clock row due at once on Sam's handle tells the barbershop, `outboxRowId` null).
- **Blocks:** `j3-block-after-threat.json` (A3-48 then S11-62: the person from the threat's read, his visit already made listed) and `j3-lift-block.json`.
- **Refused:** `invalid/refuse-j3-agent-request-without-read.json` (an AI's request with no safety read), `invalid/refuse-j3-business-door-from-agent.json` (an AI at the business's own door), `invalid/refuse-j3-read-back-yes-from-agent.json` (an AI's yes to the read-back), `invalid/refuse-j3-yes-without-way.json` (a '1 Yes' answered with neither the texts yes nor the texts-off line: `confirmBusinessBooking.response`), `invalid/refuse-j3-not-yet-left-held.json` (a '2 Not yet' that leaves the time only held: `confirmBusinessBooking.response`), `invalid/refuse-j3-turn-read-back-words.json` (a reply in a turn sending the read-back's words itself), `invalid/refuse-j3-arrival-record-without-turn.json` (the arrival record with no turn), `invalid/refuse-j3-block-from-agent.json` (an AI lifting a block), `invalid/refuse-j3-check-booking-acting-for-person.json` (the tap acting for a person) and, by the rule `arrivalTurn`, `invalid/refuse-j3-arrival-record-of-no.json` (an arrival record of a No).

Names and values come from the master's sample cast and fixture set v1's demo shops (Sam, Alex M., Riley, Omar of A3-20, Tessa and Casey of B3-08, Kim; Lee's Barbershop, Lee Home Services; October 2026), never a real person (rule 9); the safety reads are K-02 v1's own fixtures word for word, and rows and ids continue K-04's, K-28's and K-30's seeds. Phone numbers are in the 555-01xx range (+15555550142 is new here), emails on `example.com`; booking numbers, attempt ids and batch turn ids are placeholders, and arrival turn ids are `arrivalTurnId` of their event ids (default 48). The Today page's read-back words for Tessa and their sha256 are K-28's `seed-customer-business-added` words, word for word.

### Defaults used (T-050)

36. **New, the arrival record (T-246's open point; round 2: the runtime, action-layer and security reviews):** a text whose read holds danger or timed out is recorded at once by the handle actor in the text's own arrival turn, `caller.kind` actorOnArrival, so K-34 v1's `safety_outcomes_text_has_handle_and_turn` and K-04's `outbox_rows.turn_id` hold; its `turnId` and `requestId` are `arrivalTurnId(eventId)`, the SHA-256 of `arrival:` and the event id in lowercase hex, a marked input as the clock turn's is its clock row's id, so it is never the id of a batch turn (the hash of the batch's sorted event ids, CQ-09), not even the batch of that text alone, which then commits under its own id with its STOP or protecting change; a No rides in the turn its text joins (default 5).
37. **New, the first version rule:** a hold `recordSafetyOutcome` writes is at version 1 (K-34 v1 rule 2), returned as `hold.version`; the actor's at-once release names it, under the same arrival turn, with `why` `outcome_message_sent` only.
38. **N-3 (DL-029):** A3-10 and B3-15 stay in scope for the text path: a message is taken for the owner through `handOff` (kind other), a blocked customer gets none, and only the put-through call is out (C-237).
39. **Q-03:** a new customer's mobile, saved at the BOOK by `openRequest` as their own K-04 handle, gets its contacts/add effect then, and the owner's chat link for the customer comes only once the add has worked (C-254).
40. **Q-09 and CQ-13:** an AI's request opens for the person its token is bound to, or, for an unbound token, a new nameless person the Agent API keeps with the token (CQ-13: unbound, search, times, prices, save a time; bound, book and read that customer's bookings); booking on an AI's request needs the tie first (Q-09: a code the customer types back, by C9's route), which is K-20's and journeys 4 to 6's; an AI's call is never the customer's own yes (A3-03).
41. **New, the request's booker:** the server names it (the caller's own person, a customer on the business's own list, or a new person it writes), never the browser or the AI; a booking the business adds names the customer as booker, person served and payer, as K-28 v1's `seed-customer-business-added` does.
42. **New, when the request is written:** once the door's words read as a new booking (A3-51, B3-40), after their danger check, as K-30 v1 says (`booking_details`: "written with the head when the request comes in (A3-51, B3-40; the owner's BOOK, A3-19, B3-08; a walk-in, A3-20)"); A3-51's other branches open no request.
43. **New, the BOOK's values are stored in the BOOK turn, and the reply books them (round 2: action-layer FAILs 1 and 3, AI FAIL 1):** the values the BOOK words or the Today page entry name are taken where their words are, as decide's part fields each re-parsed from the BOOK words with that text's send time under limit 6 (arch 03 line 185; arch 06 line 309: a read-back is re-parsed only for a photo), and stored by `openRequest` where A3-30's read-back and its reply read them as facts with versions: the service as the K-30 line written on the owner's BOOK (K-30 README), a new customer's contacts as their own K-04 handles (which K-28's row names), and the time entered held for the booking by K-05 v1 reserve (held, the standard 10 minutes: Q-12) right after the BOOK's commit, since the hold names the head. The reply confirms what is stored and books it as entered on '1 Yes' and '2 Not yet' alike: the hold confirmed booked through K-05 v1 commitTurn, the head's stage booked, `booking_details.times` set once (A3-30: "The reply books it, as entered", MASTER-FLOW-A line 1956, B line 1746; A4-98; A4-14, A5-31; K-30 README: the commit that writes the booking includes A3-30). A hold that lapsed before the reply is limit 5's (nextOpenTimes; on the page `timeTaken`). The booked time lives on K-04's reservations (K-30 README "Times").
44. **New, '2 Not yet' still gives its keys and books:** the customer row, the way their contacts imply (K-28 `customers_way_fits_contact`), who it is for and the time are written and the booking is made on either answer; only the consent and the link wait (A3-40: "Rosie texts the customer nothing"; "The booking is made").
45. **New, A3-43's code:** a K-22 `link_record` code (as limit 1's `unprovenSender`) to the booking's contact on file, by the email on file or a text into that mobile's open chat, never back to the page (MC C9; open gap 17), with the same answer whether or not a booking matched.
46. **New, A3-41, A3-12 and A3-45 in a turn:** each is the turn's reply row; A3-41's message for the business and A3-10's message taken are v1's `handOff`; A3-12's 'Wrong number' in reply to a text is a stop for that number (`recordStopOrStart`, from the phone's own text: RULES 7.2); A3-45 writes nothing more. A3-41's reply row, and A3-43's in a text (default 54), are composed from a `composeInput` that carries no booking fact (no booking, time, service or name of one), so their words are the same whether or not a booking exists (MASTER-FLOW-A lines 1796, 1809; round 2, AI note g); C-114 and C-118 build them so, and A3-43's page link waits for its K-03 slot (K-03's minor version or K-21's journey 3 version, T-060).
47. **New, the block's person:** found from a customer row of the business or a threat's safety read, never sent by the page; the number blocked is their own mobile (`customer_blocks_phone_own_fk`).
48. **New, placeholders:** booking numbers, attempt ids, batch turn ids (standing for CQ-09's hashes), `res-casey` (no frozen seed holds Lee Home Services' workers yet), line, reservation and open question ids, the fixed line ids `safetyUnsure` and `textsOffReachTheirWay` (K-01's, their words waiting on Q-01: open gap 8), the block's read-back words (S11-62 prints none) and every time of day the steps do not print are examples. Arrival turn ids are real: `arrivalTurnId` of their event ids, which the rule checks.
49. **New, a listed customer's contacts come from the row (round 2: frontend FAIL 1, action-layer note c):** for a customer already on the list, `confirmBusinessBooking` takes the contacts and the way from their K-28 row and the request carries none, so a page that found them by name needs no number, and `findCustomer` still never returns one; contacts are entered only for a new customer, at the BOOK (`newCustomerEntered`), and a typed number Rosie already holds is linked as that person's own handle, so no answer tells the business that Rosie knew it (round 2: security note e; RULES 4.9, 6.5).
50. **New, STOP's notice to the businesses (v1's open gap 18; round 2: security FAIL 1):** each business with an open booking for a stopped phone hears only "Texts to this customer are off. Reach them their way." (RULES 7.5; MC C1; S13-32, MASTER-FLOW-A line 7229; A3-12), never where the stop came from: on the STOP record (`recordStopOrStart.response.businessesTold`, one row to each such business's owner in the same commit), and when '1 Yes' meets a stopped mobile (`confirmBusinessBooking.response.textsOff`: no consent and no link, since only START or a fresh yes sent from that number turns texts back on, S13-32 line 7242; security note a). An open booking is one at stage entry or booked whose booker, person served or payer has that phone as their own handle. Round 3 (security FAILs 1 and 2, action-layer FAIL 2 of round 2): a call with no turn writes no outbox row (open gap 43), so on the Today page the texts-off line has no row (`textsOff.outboxRowId` null; the page shows it), and a STOP made with no turn tells each business through default 56.
51. **New, a limit on `checkBookingFirst`'s codes (round 2: security note f):** a contact on file gets at most one live code from the tap: while one lives (10 minutes) or its 15-minute pause holds (RULES 6.2), a new tap sends nothing and answers the same `sentIfMatched`, so new request ids never flood a customer and the answer still never tells whether a booking exists.
52. **New, the Today page's read-back (round 2: frontend FAIL 2, security note c):** `readBackBusinessBooking` answers the page server the read-back's exact words, their sha256 and the versions they were built from; the words are code's (A3-30's read-back line, its K-03 v1 slots filled from the stored facts: svc.intext from the line's service, day, visit.time and change.when from the held reservation in the visit's zone, arrive (flow B's arrival window, B3-08's 'arriving 12-2pm') from the held reservation too, its start to its end less the line's service length (K-25 v1 services.length; K-30 README "Times": the reserved time also holds the job's length; round 2, AI note a), pro.name from its resource, the customer's name, age band and contact from their row or the BOOK's handles, two.count), so the same versions give the same words, and the confirm refills and compares them. The page server keeps them with its session; the browser never sends them. In a turn the read-back is the BOOK turn's reply (C-118 composes it from the same facts, which now exist: AI FAIL 1) and its open question holds the words and versions the reply names.
53. **New, '3 Change it' (round 2: AI FAIL 1):** `confirmBusinessBooking` with `changeIt` takes the changed values in the turn or page call that holds them, re-parsed there under limit 6, and stores them as the BOOK's were (the old hold released and the new time held, the line replaced, a BOOK-added customer's name and contacts corrected); it gives no key and books nothing, and the booking is read back again. A part of the time the change does not name (the day, the person) is the held reservation's, never resolved from the reply's send time, so '3, make it 11:30' keeps Sat Oct 24 and Riley (`j3-confirm-business-booking-change.json`); decide's field and limit 6's re-parse both read the unnamed parts from the hold (round 2, AI note e). A listed customer's name and contacts are not changed here (A7-98; S13-45 row 3).
54. **New, the doors A3-51 and A3-43 (round 2: AI note 2):** v1's `enterDoor` names S11-12, S11-21 and S11-42 and leaves A3-51 and A3-43 to a later minor version; v1.1 keeps `enterDoor`'s list as it is and routes both as decided parts: A3-51's branches are decide's choice in the turn (its 'A new booking' is `openRequest`), and A3-43's text reply is the turn's reply row with the page's tap `checkBookingFirst`. C-076 and C-115 build them so.
55. **New, what a booking the business adds took (round 2: action-layer FAIL 1):** `openRequest` at door business writes K-30 `booking_details.taken` 'nothing' once, in the details row's insert in the BOOK's commit (by text or on the Today page), since a booking the business adds takes nothing: K-30 v1's own column names it ("nothing (... a booking the business adds)", `bookings-changes.v1.tables.json` line 86), SPEC-master 5e line 220 ("a shop-added booking") and A3-30 ("It's booked as entered: nothing is taken unless the customer adds a card on the link", MASTER-FLOW-A line 1961). So A3-30's and B3-08's '2 Not yet', whose gate shows only when taken is 'nothing' (master-A.json A3-30, lines 9010 to 9014; MASTER-FLOW-A line 1976; MASTER-FLOW-B line 1767), is on the reply's frontier, since a record gate passes only when the booking holds the value (arch 03 line 161) and a null reads as the first value, asked (SPEC-master line 220). How it agrees with K-30's "Null until the money step": that line is for the customer's own doors (ai, page, chat, walk_in), whose money step (A5-04: "the one place that sets what this booking took"; A5-08) sets it; a booking the business adds has no money step (A3-30: "The chat's questions are skipped on this path"), so its BOOK is where taken is decided, once, and it stays 'nothing' (SPEC-master 5e: "What the booking took stays as it was"); a card the customer adds later on the link is K-32's payment record, not a change of taken. `confirmBusinessBooking` never writes it.
56. **New, a STOP made with no turn tells each business (round 2: security FAIL 2):** a STOP made alone, with no turn (a page's or an email's STOP for the caller's own number, `inboundEventId` null; or a STOP text its handle actor writes on return while PostgreSQL was down, `caller.kind` actorOnReturn, as `start-recorded.json` writes a START), commits under `caller.requestId` and cannot write an outbox row (K-04 v1 `outbox_rows.turn_id` is not null). So its commit writes, for each business with an open booking for that phone (default 50), one K-04 v1 `clock_rows` row of kind `step` (`fires` naming S13-32's notice and the fixed line `textsOffReachTheirWay`), owned by that business's owner's handle (`handle_id`), with the business and one of those bookings, armed and due at once in the business's zone. Its firing is a clock turn on the owner's handle (default 24: its one event id the clock row's id), whose commit writes the notice as a row to the owner, send class routine, held for the owner's text hours as any owner row is, never saying where the stop came from. Each business is told within the minute (the owner's actor fires a row due at once, else the minute sweep fires it as missed: arch 10 line 540), and the stopped phone is never texted: the clock turn runs on the owner's handle and writes only the owner's row. `businessesTold` names each business with `outboxRowId` null (`j3-stop-on-return-tells-businesses.json`). A STOP in a turn writes the rows in its own commit, as before.

### Open gaps (v1.1)

27. **A business that serves both flows:** `openRequest` takes the flow of the door's step graph (A3-01 gives A, B3-01 gives B); for a business whose kinds go both ways, a request with no service yet (A4-81 is v1.2's) has no flow in the sources. Default (round 2: action-layer note g, for C-110 and the booking page): at such a business a request at door ai or page opens in flow A, A3-01's step graph, and a service named later whose way is flow B's is K-09 v1.2's service match (A4-81) to route. For QUESTIONS.md. Closed by v1.2 round 2: `matchService`'s place pick moves the request to the other flow (default 75).
28. **K-20 is not frozen** (QUESTIONS.md M0-M1.2: K-09 v1.1 first, then K-20, T-029): the agent token, its binding and how the Agent API names `caller.personId` are named in words.
29. **K-21's journey 3 version (T-060):** how the booking page and the Today page name their events (`yesEventId`; open gap 20) and keep the booker, the attempt id and the read-back's words and versions (default 52) with their session, so none of them comes from the browser.
30. **K-32 money is not frozen** (F16): S11-62's "A plan or membership ends with the block" and its credits; v1.1 lists the bookings already made, and plans, memberships and credits come with K-32 and K-09 v1.3.
31. **K-08 kinds for journey 3's writes** (CQ-56): K-08 v1.1 has `booking.created` and `booking.stage_changed`, but no kind for a request opened at a door, a customer added or a block set or lifted; CQ-56's default (K-08 adds each kind with the version that first writes it) applies, before C-099 and C-105 commit them.
32. **A paused person's request** (A3-48: "New requests from that phone or place wait for Sam"): v1.1 answers `waitsForOwner` and writes nothing; whether the request is kept for Sam to see is not in the sources. For QUESTIONS.md.
33. **A message that can't wait, links held, repeated asks, and "only Rosie's team gets it"** (A3-41): K-36 v1's hand-off of kind other waits for the owner's text hours and has no place for a message only Rosie's team gets or for its held links; v1.1 uses v1's `handOff`, and a K-36 or K-37 minor version adds them. For QUESTIONS.md.
34. **Which turn an arrival record's release re-decides:** K-34 v1 gives a hold one turn, the arrival turn, whose only part is the safety record; the text's routine parts are its batch turn's, under its own id (default 36), held while the hold is open, which the actor maps to the release (C-046). A K-34 minor version could name the held batch turn on the hold.
35. **A child's texts** (A3-45: "keeps nothing: no name, no number and no recording"): the gateway already stored the text sealed under the handle's key; whether it is erased at once is not in the sources (RULES 4.7, 4.10). For QUESTIONS.md.
36. **A booking the business adds for someone else** (a child, another adult): v1.1's read-back is for the customer named, who is booker, person served and payer; who-for steps for another person are A4-80's (v1.2).
37. **The read-back's other values** (A3-30: the language, an access need such as an interpreter, relay or more time, and "Private" in the reply): the language is K-04 `persons.language`; an access need and the interpreter to-do have no frozen home; "Private" is `setRecordFlag` (v1).
38. **The walk-in's business from the door sign** (A3-20: "Omar texts the number on the door sign"): one number serves every business, and K-29 v1's chat links have no door-sign purpose; how the sign names the business is open.
39. **The service match for a customer's own request** (A4-81) is v1.2's. At the business's BOOK, v1.1 matches the words as typed to one of the business's own services and its way for the request's flow and copies the K-30 line then (K-30 README: `booking_lines` written "on the owner's BOOK"); words that fit none or several are limit 6's one question. How code matches the words (the names on the list, or the meaning index of Q-42) is C-099's, with no source naming it.
40. **The same customer twice** (S12-32): a typed number Rosie already holds is linked as the new person's own handle (default 49), so no `constraint` answer tells the business that Rosie knew it; whether the two are one customer is journey 12's path (v1.10).
41. **A new customer's age band and way at the BOOK:** they have no frozen home before the K-28 row, which is the reply's (customer, reach_by, who_for: one writer per key). Default: the read-back says the way the stored contacts imply (texts with a mobile, else email, else none: K-28 `customers_way_fits_contact`) and "18 or older", and the reply writes them so; another age band is A4-80's who-for steps (v1.2; open gap 36) and another way the customer's own word (A7-98). A K-28 minor version (a customer row not yet confirmed) or a K-04 one (values on an open question) would keep entered ones. For QUESTIONS.md.
42. **The visit row and terms snapshot of a booking the business adds:** K-30 README writes `booking_terms` in A3-30's commit and `booking_visits` "when the booking is written", but journey 3's sources give such a booking no ticked terms, cancel rule shown or check-in code. Default: the reply's commit books the reservation and sets the stage (what holds the time), and the visit and snapshot are written by the K-09 minor version that writes a booking's visit and terms (v1.4, A6-10's), which the database engineer checks against K-30's nullability (`terms_consent_id`, `check_in_code`). For QUESTIONS.md.
43. **CQ-44, restated for journey 3** (action-layer note a): every v1.1 call made alone commits under `caller.requestId` and waits for K-05's commit with no turn (open gap 3): doors ai and page (A3-02, A3-04, B3-02, B3-07), the Today page's `openRequest`, `confirmBusinessBooking` and their reserve, `checkBookingFirst`, the Customers page's `setCustomerBlock`, and the page's and the AI's safety reads; a page's commit writes no outbox row (K-04 `outbox_rows.turn_id` is not null), so the page shows its own result. CQ-44's default holds until K-05 adds it. Round 3 (security FAILs 1 and 2, action-layer FAIL 2 of round 2): so the Today page's texts-off line has no row (`confirmBusinessBooking.response.textsOff.outboxRowId` null, the page showing the line: default 50), and a STOP made with no turn (a page's or an email's STOP for the caller's own number, or one its actor writes on return) tells each business with an open booking through a clock row due at once on the owner's handle, whose clock turn writes the notice within the minute and never texts the stopped phone (`recordStopOrStart.response.businessesTold[].outboxRowId` null: default 56).
44. **The K-05 reads journey 3's builders need** (action-layer note b): K-05 v1.2 reads facts by primary key only; journey 3 also reads customers by mobile or name, a customer by business and booker, open blocks by number, person or address, a booker's pauses, open safety holds (K-05 open gap 26), a booking by name and day (A3-43), a booking's held reservation, the bookings open for a phone (default 50) and a person's booked bookings; and (round 2: action-layer note a, AI note d) a text's safety outcome by its event id, for the batch turn's repeated record (K-34 `safety_outcomes` has no version column, so K-05's state read cannot read it; meanwhile the action layer rebuilds the first record from its fixed ids and retries the commit after `safety_outcomes_event_key` refuses it), resources and services by name with the price rule in force (the BOOK's matching), a person's handles (a new customer's mobile and email), a business owner's handle (`businessesTold` and default 56's clock rows), a contact's live codes (default 51), and a resource's open times after a time (limit 5's next open times at the BOOK). A K-05 minor version (v1.3) adds them before C-099's, C-094's and C-105's journey 3 tasks; until then those tasks wait (NEED), with no default read.
45. **S11-62's command by text:** the owner's 'block Kim' and a typed address come in the command's turn and `setCustomerBlock` runs on the read-back's yes; as at the BOOK, the values belong in the command's turn. Default: in a turn the call takes `who` by the row ids the read-back's open question read (a customer row or a safety outcome: ids, not words) and an address block only from the Customers page, whose words come from the page server (default 52's rule), until a K-09 minor version (v1.2 at the earliest, with journey 4) stores a pending block as v1.1 stores the BOOK's values. For QUESTIONS.md.
46. **'3 Change it' and the BOOK's line:** K-30 v1 lets a line's `state`, `removed_at` and `removed_by_change_id` change, and a change row is after booking; default: a line replaced before the booking is set removed with no change row (`removed_by_change_id` null), which the database engineer confirms against K-30's constraints (C-166).

From the second verification's notes (round 3), each with its default. For QUESTIONS.md:

47. **The age band in the read-back** (AI note b): arch 07 line 409 has a read-back carry each value its yes sets as a slot and match each number to a code fact, and A3-30's yes sets the age (MC C6), but K-03 v1 has no age-band slot, so default 52's age band has no slot to fill and C-118's read-back in a turn could fail the Values check on '18'. Default: the read-back states only the band v1.1 writes at the BOOK, "18 or older" (open gap 41), in A3-30's master words with no slot, and a K-03 minor version (with K-01's slot list) adds an age-band slot before journey 3's composer task (C-118).
48. **'3 Change it' naming an age band or a way, and the read-back's language and access need** (AI note c): `confirmBusinessBooking.changed` takes no age band and no way, so '3, she's 16' reaches no call, and open gap 37 gives the language and an access need no default (K-03 v1 has no slot for either). Default: a change that names only an age band or a way stores nothing and books nothing (A3-30's safe default: "nothing is booked until it's confirmed"); the reply asks once what '3 Change it' can change here (the time, the person, the service, or a BOOK-added customer's name and contacts), another age band is A4-80's who-for steps (v1.2) and another way the customer's own word (A7-98). In v1.1 the read-back carries no language or access need: the language stays K-04 `persons.language` as stored, and an access need waits for its frozen home and a K-03 slot.
49. **Answer-field names for the BOOK and '3 Change it'** (AI note f): K-01 v1's `answerField` name is a gives key or a record value, and A3-19 gives only request, so the customer's name, the service, the time with the person, a mobile and an email have no K-01 name for C-058, C-075 and the gold set (C-140). Default: they are named by this contract's own fields (`openRequest.booker.name`, `enteredBooking.time` with `resourceId`, `during` and `zone`, `enteredBooking.service.asTyped`, `newCustomerEntered.mobile` and `.email`, and the same names under `confirmBusinessBooking.changed`) until a K-01 minor version lists them as answer fields.
50. **The BOOK's hold named before it exists** (action-layer note b): in a turn the read-back's open question, with its words and versions, is committed in the BOOK's commit, before K-05 reserve runs (the hold names the head), so the action layer names the hold by an id it chooses before the commit. Default: that id is passed to reserve; if reserve answers overlap, the read-back still names the time entered, and a '1' or '2' to it fails safely at limit 5 (nextOpenTimes), nothing booked, while '3 Change it' takes a new time. No fixture covers it; QA's T-117 may add one.
51. **`checkBookingFirst`'s code from a page** (action-layer note c): the code goes to the contact on file as an outbox row (default 45), which a page's commit with no turn cannot write (open gap 43; K-05 open gap 3). Default: until CQ-44's commit with no turn and a K-04 minor version let a call made alone write an outbox row, the tap writes and sends nothing and answers `sentIfMatched` as always, which still tells no one whether a booking exists; A3-43 by text (the turn's reply row) is not affected.
52. **A '2 Not yet' booking's visit row, and B3-24's address** (action-layer note d): open gap 42's default (the visit row and terms snapshot written by v1.4, A6-10's) covers '1 Yes', whose walk goes on, but not '2 Not yet', which ends in journey 3 (A3-40, B3-24), so no v1.1 call writes that booking's visit row; and B3-24's "The address from the call is added on the Today page" (MASTER-FLOW-B line 1775) has no call. Default: a '2 Not yet' booking is held by its booked reservation and its stage until the K-09 minor version that writes a booking's visit and terms (v1.4) writes them for it too, and the address waits for the K-09 minor version with the booking's address call (B4-30, B4-77: v1.2); until then the business tells the customer the details itself (B3-24).
53. **'1 Yes' for a customer with no contact** (action-layer note e): it is refused before any write with `notAllowed`, but none of v1's four `why` values (notDecider, unprovenSender, wrongRole, otherBusiness) fits. Default: `why` notDecider, since with no contact there is no number for which the business's word can count (S13-32, MASTER-FLOW-A line 7215), until a K-09 minor version adds a value of its own.

## v1.2 (T-051): journey 4

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): 02-contracts K-09's version table, row v1.2 ("match a listed service (one 'which?'; not on the list; can't be booked); where; who it is for and age; the travel area (B4-77); rule-outs, price and length questions; repeat, group, several services, plan; needs; the reach card (a mobile required: C2; contact added first: Q-03); the name; the price way (list; photos with the owner's OK; at the visit); what is booked; the times card (search: five, soonest then nearest, exact prices: arch 05); the pick (a shown time, a named person, another day, the waitlist, next in line, sent now); save a time (10 minutes; 15 for C3's door, never extended); who is who; the secure link; the street address; who meets the pro; forms; book on the yes or ask the owner first; notices; page answers"), frozen before journey 4's first task (section 9, F16); v1.1's pointers to it (section "v1.1": A4-14, A4-23, A4-80, A4-82 and twins; open gaps 27, 36, 39, 41, 45, 52); and, in round 2, the first verification's points as the lead's nine rulings fold them (`ops/cards/T-051.md`, "Round 2 rulings", DL-162; section "Round 2" below), and in round 3 the second verification's as the lead's three rulings fold them (`ops/cards/T-051.md`, "Round 3 rulings", DL-171; section "Round 3" below), and in round 4, in this README alone, the third verification's point as the lead's three rulings fold it (`ops/cards/T-051.md`, "Round 4 rulings", DL-175; section "Round 4" below). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `tool-api.v1.2.schema.json` holds every v1.1 definition under its own name: the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.1.schema.json`, which stays as it is and refers to v1; the changed ones (`stepKey`, `given`, `partResult`, `gateAndCommit.committed`, `gateAndCommit.response`, `gateAndCommit.exchange`, `callName`) take only new enum values. So every v1 and v1.1 exchange is a v1.2 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every v1 and v1.1 fixture against v1.2). **Scope:** A4-01 to A4-99 and B4-01 to B4-115 (A4-87 and B4-109, 'Finishes the booking on the call', are out: C-231, C-232), with S12-01 to S12-05 and S12-28. K-16 v1 (busy blocks, read through K-27), K-17 v1, K-27 v1 and K-30 v1, frozen on main, are named by table, column and call, never redefined; K-28 v1 as v1.1 names it.

### The calls v1.2 adds

Every call reads PostgreSQL's **primary, uncached** (arch 02 line 117; arch 10 lines 504, 538), runs under v1.1's `businessCaller` (a business always named; row-level security scopes every row: arch 10 line 546; K-30 v1 rule 5), names its person on the server, never from the browser's or an AI's words (K-22 v1 rule 4), takes `caller.requestId` as the key a repeat is known by (a turn's id in a turn; a repeat writes nothing and answers the first result), and can also answer `transient`. In a turn each write is the chosen choice's effect inside `gateAndCommit`, checked by limits 1 to 6, and its committed part names the keys (`partResult.gives`); an exchange with `caller.kind` turn shows that write as it rides there. A call made alone (the booking page, the secure page, the pricing page, the customer's own AI) commits under `caller.requestId`, waits for K-05's commit with no turn (CQ-44; v1.1 open gap 43) and writes no outbox row. Callers are held by the schema (`dependentSchemas`; `$defs/customerSideCaller`: turn, page or agent).

| Call              | Gives (steps)                                                                                                                                                         | Callers                                                                                                                              | Writes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

No source gives these calls a time budget: in a turn each runs inside `gateAndCommit`'s 300 ms (arch 04 line 223); `showTimes` alone at most 200 ms proposed (the 'Search, one indexed query' budget, arch 04 line 217, as v1 default 3); the rest none (open gap 11). Each request description in the schema states the call in full with its sources: its writes, version rule, repeat and errors. New errors: `wrongDetails` (S12-05), `overBookingLimit` (S12-28), `quoteState`, `notReachable` (no contact, or a chat not open: MC C9), `mobileRequired` (MC C2), `geoUnavailable` (K-17 v1's errors), and in round 2 `savedTimeCap` (K-05 reserve's, with only when the caller's own two saved times end) and `extendTime`'s K-05 extend errors (`neverExtended`, `alreadyExtended`, `notHeld`), and in round 3 `extendTime`'s `notExtendable` (a time held for an agent token or a booking, refused before K-05 extend) and `saveTime`'s `mobileRequired` (a page's or a turn's save with no mobile on file to hold it for). Shared shapes added in round 2: `optionPrice`, `linkIssued` (every link a call issues, how it reaches its reader and its K-03 slot) and `textsOffNotice`.

who_for, reach_by, times and customer at a booking the business adds stay journey 3's `confirmBusinessBooking` (A3-30, B3-08); at journey 4's own steps they are these calls, so within each journey each key has exactly one call. Each request description names its keys with their step ids, and each response answers the keys it wrote in `gives`, typed per call. The named rule `givesJourneyFourKeys` checks every fixture that states a journey 4 step: made alone, it writes exactly that step's keys, with those of the automatic step its own call passes (the save after the pick), by the key's one call; in a turn, its step's keys and any of those the automatic steps its walk passes give (`J4_STEP_GIVES`, `J4_CALL_PASSES`, `J4_WALK_PASSES`, `J4_KEY_CALL`; round 2).

### Named rule added (`tool-api.rules.mjs`)

v1's and v1.1's six rules stay as they are; v1.2 adds one under a new name, with its tables exported beside it.

- **`givesJourneyFourKeys`:** an exchange whose `given.stated` names a journey 4 step writes the keys that step gives and its outcome wrote exactly the stated keys (`J4_STEP_GIVES`, from A-J4.md and B-J4.md). Made alone, it states exactly its step's keys with those of the automatic steps its own call passes (`J4_CALL_PASSES`: the pick then the save), less time_saved for 'Send someone now' (B4-91: saveTime answers choice alone, open gap 61), and its call is the one call that writes each key (`J4_KEY_CALL`). In a turn (`gateAndCommit`) a part states its step's own keys and may add those of the automatic steps its walk passes (`J4_WALK_PASSES`: after the pick the save and the secure link, after the owner's send or the read-back's yes the quote sent), as v1's `givesStepKeys` allows (round 2: the action-layer engineer's note a, the QA and eval engineer's note a: `j4-save-time-turn.json` and `j4-secure-link-turn.json` share one turn, so its part gives choice, time_saved and link).

The rules file's name carries no version, so the frozen-contract guard does not protect it (STATE note 25's class): QA's contract test (T-118) and the guard's tooling task pin it.

### Defaults used (T-051)

57. **New, a value given before its home row exists (round-1 lesson of T-050: values are stored where their words are, then read as facts with versions):** K-30 v1 writes the booking line only when the time is saved (its writer table: A4-21, B4-23), and K-28 v1's customer row needs the name A4-23 gives, after A4-80 and A4-82. So the matched service and way (A4-81, A4-01, B4-01, B4-02; round 2: the way picked by `matchService`'s place pick, default 75), the list or visit price (A4-06, A4-13, B4-07, B4-14), and for a customer with no K-28 row yet the age band (A4-80) and the way (A4-82) are kept until their row is written: in a turn, as the answered option of the K-04 open question that asked (`open_questions.answered_option_id`), and the walk's next open question's options and `versions_read`, which name the K-25 service and way rows; from the booking page or an AI, with the page server's session or the Agent API's token session (K-21, K-20), never the browser's or the AI's words, checked again against the list and its versions by the call that writes the home. The save writes the line (`saveTime`) and `addCustomer` the row; `setWhoFor` and `setReachWay` write K-28 directly when the row exists. A K-30 and K-28 minor version giving these values a column of their own is open gap 54.
58. **New, who it's for:** the head's person served and payer are named by the server: the booker, or a new K-04 person named as typed for someone else; never a person id the browser or the AI sends (K-22 v1 rule 4).
59. **Q-07, the reach card:** texts, textsonly or email; calls only is not offered; textsonly works like texts while there is no voice; a mobile is required when the customer or their own AI books (MC C2, `mobileRequired`); a customer with only a landline is booked by the business (A3-19).
60. **New, A4-80's ending branches:** under 13 and alone, a teen booking alone, adults only for a child and a booker not allowed give no key and keep nothing about the child (RULES 4.7); the request ends with K-30 request_ended_why dropped, since its list has no reason of its own for them (open gap 66).
61. **Q-12 and MC C3, saved times (round 2, DL-162 ruling 4; round 3, DL-171 ruling 1):** a saved time is on one of K-05 v1's two doors. ownAi, 15 minutes as a calendar reservation, never extended, only for the customer's own AI booking a service that books at once with money due now or a card to save (MC C3; K-05 v1 door ownAi): `caller.kind` agent, the K-26 v1 OK rules A4-98 reads (ok_same_day, ok_new_customer, ok_dates) saying it books on the yes, and the way's path taking a card, a deposit or the full price before. Every other save is standard, every caller's request the owner OKs included ('A request the owner OKs keeps the master's own rules'): 10 minutes with one 10-minute extension offered once (`extendTime`, K-05 extend; Q-12; A4-21). An AI's booking with nothing to pay or save before is booked at once on its request by the booking write (D104; A6-10, K-09 v1.4), and v1.2 holds its time on the standard door until then. saveTime reads the OK rules A4-98 then writes from, so the door and the approval agree. **Who it is held for, one rule per caller on either door (round 3; action-layer GAP 1, runtime note a):** an agent caller's save is held for its agent token (K-05 v1 savedFor.agentToken), which the Tool API takes from the agent's own authentication (K-20, C-110), never from a field the caller fills (no request carries one, and `businessCaller` has none), and arch 05 line 247's '2 per agent token per business' counts it (K-05 item 8); a page's or a turn's save is held for the booker's mobile on file (K-05 v1 savedFor.phoneNumber; with none on file, `mobileRequired`, MC C2), so its saved-time clock row is that mobile's handle's (K-05 item 8) and S12-04 can go into an open chat (MC C9). **The extension, from a stored fact, never the caller's word:** `extendTime` reads who the time is held for (K-04 reservations.for_phone_number, for_agent_token or booking_id). Held for the booker's mobile, it was a page's or a turn's save, always on the standard door (`saveTime.exchange`), so `extendTime` names standard to K-05 extend and answers heldFor phoneNumber. Held for an agent token, no frozen field records its door (K-04 v1 keeps none on the row, as K-05 v1 extend.request says; K-30 v1 booking_details.door is the booking's door, not the hold's; booking_details.approval is decided at A4-98, after the save, from rules that may change in between: open gap 84), so `extendTime` refuses it before K-05 extend (`notExtendable`, heldFor agentToken), nothing changed, until a K-05 or K-30 minor version records the hold's door (open gap 85), and `saveTime` answers an agent caller's save extendable false on either door. That fails closed: C3's 15 minutes are never extended, and an AI's standard save is not extended until the door is recorded. A booking's hold (only the business's own BOOK holds one: v1.1 openRequest at door business) is not a saved time `extendTime` extends (`notExtendable`, heldFor booking). **The lapse:** a page's or a turn's save lapses by its mobile's handle's clock turn, which sends S12-04's one text only into an open chat (MC C9); an agent caller's save lapses by the minute sweep, whose firing has no turn and writes no outbox row (K-05 open gap 3; CQ-44), and since nothing records its door it texts nobody on either door: on C3's ownAi door as MC C3 says ('nobody is chased, so S12-04's text is not sent'), and for an AI's standard save until the door is recorded (open gap 85). `saveTime.exchange` holds a turn's and a page's save to the standard door, and an agent caller's standard save to extendable false.
62. **New, how a secure link goes:** by text only into an open chat (RULES 4.1b; MC C9), or to the email on file, both in a turn as an outbox row whose final parts hold the link; to the customer's own AI or on the booking page only in the answer's url, once; from a page's commit, through a clock row (default 72); never by text from a call made alone (v1.1 open gap 43). Only the code's sha256 is kept, and the link never enters a span, a trace or the log (v1 default 30); a repeat answers with no url (K-30 default 12: a new message needs a new link). Round 2: every link a call issues answers as `linkIssued` (the photos link by `quoteFromPhotos`' via, see first's times link, the quote link, a teen's parent link), naming its K-03 slot or open gap 70.
63. **New, A4-43 and A4-98 ride the link's commit:** they are automatic steps right after A4-42 and B4-29 (arch 03 line 191), so `sendSecureLink` writes the forms' outcome and booking_details.approval, decided once from K-26 v1's OK rules.
64. **New, S12-28 and S12-05:** a booking over the plug-in's limit of open bookings for a phone or an address is refused (`overBookingLimit`, no reason to the caller) and Rosie's team gets an S12-28 review item through v1's `openReviewItem`; details an AI or a page sends that are not today's list are refused (`wrongDetails`), nothing held.
65. **New, the price the line copies:** the exact price the times card showed for that time and person (arch 05; S11-54), which can differ from A4-06's list price for a staff price or a dated rule; the line's copy is never changed after (K-30 rule 6).
66. **New, the owner's quote question from a page:** a price made on the pricing page writes no outbox row (v1.1 open gap 43), so its commit arms a K-04 clock row due at once on the owner's handle whose clock turn writes the question, as v1.1 default 56 does for a STOP.
67. **New, the street address only from the secure page:** an address never passes through an AI (A6-14; B4-30), so `checkPlace` street takes only the page, for the link's own person; the town or ZIP may come from any of the customer's ways. A null state, a town two states share or none found asks for the ZIP (B4-77; K-17 v1 default 8).
68. **Q-03:** a new mobile from the reach card gets its contacts/add effect when it is saved; a number Rosie already holds is linked as this person's own handle too, so no answer tells whether Rosie knew it (v1.1 default 49).
69. **New, the times card in a turn:** its open question holds the times as options and the rows the card was built from in `versions_read`; the pick names the option, and the action layer rebuilds the card from those versions (the same versions give the same times, as v1.1 default 52's words), so a row that moved fails limit 4 and a time taken limit 5.
70. **CQ-13 and Q-09, an AI's token:** an unbound token may match a service, see prices and times and save a time; the calls that write a person's contacts, their customer row or their link (`setReachWay`, `addCustomer`, `sendSecureLink` via ai) need the token bound to that person (`caller.personId` set: Q-09's tie, K-20's), which the schema holds (`refuse-j4-reach-card-unbound-agent.json`). Round 2 (security note d): `quoteFromPhotos` askPhotos from an AI needs it bound too, since it makes a link for that person.

### Round 2 (DL-162): what changed

The first verification (round 1, b1a802c) had QA's, the runtime engineer's and the QA and eval engineer's PASS and four FAILs (the action-layer engineer's 7 gaps, the AI engineer's 2, the frontend engineer's 5, the security reviewer's 2), which the lead folded into nine rulings (`ops/cards/T-051.md`). Each is fixed in v1.2 itself, still only adding to v1.1 (every v1 and v1.1 fixture still passes v1.2), and every round 1 PASS point stands (the bound-token rules, page-only street addresses and answers, only the code's sha256 kept, url only to an AI or a page and null in a turn, no v1.2 call writing a charge, a Stripe effect or a provider event, an AI unable to price or send a quote, C3's 15 minutes never extended, `businessCaller` and row-level security everywhere).
